\gamma\gtrsim\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-2}\beta_{\textup{RSS}}^{2}\kappa^{4}
, and other conditions in Theorem
2
hold. Then, after
I
-th iteration of Algorithm
2
with

I\gtrsim\log(\kappa^{-1}\sigma_{1}^{-1/3}g_{\min})/\log(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{2}\beta_{\textup{RSS}}^{-2}\kappa^{-4})
, with probability at least
1-4\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-2},\tau^{-4})T]-2\exp[-C\log(p)]
,

\|\mathbf{A}^{(I)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}\lesssim\kappa\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{[sr+r^{2}+s\min(\log(p),\log(ep/s))]/T}.

(6.2)

From the above theorem, the estimation efficiency of the sparsity-constrained estimator is improved significantly. Specifically, when both
s
and
r
are much smaller than
p
, the required sample size is reduced from
T\gtrsim p
to
T\gtrsim s\log(p)
. In other words, the proposed sparsity-constrained method can be applied to the case with
T\ll p
.

7
Simulation Studies

7.1
VAR with common factors

We conduct two simulation experiments to evaluate the finite-sample performance of the proposed estimation methods. The number of replications is set to 500 for each experiment.

In the first experiment, the VAR(1) model in (
2.7
) is considered with
r=3
,
d=0,1,2,3
, and
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}=\mathbf{I}_{p}
.
The dimension is
p=40
or 100, and we consider
T\in\{500,600,700,800\}
for
p=40
and
T\in\{1000,1200,1400,1600\}
for
p=100
.
The orthonormal matrices
\mathbf{C},\mathbf{R},\mathbf{P}
and
\mathbf{O}_{1},\mathbf{O}_{2}\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}
are generated randomly in each replication such that
\mathbf{C}^{\top}\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{C}^{\top}\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{0}_{d\times(r-d)}
.
Moreover, let
\mathbf{S}=\text{diag}(c_{1},c_{2},c_{3})
with each
c_{i}\sim_{i.i.d.}\text{Unif}(0.8,1.5)
in each replication.
As a result, from (
2.1
) and (
2.7
), the parameter matrix assumes the form
\mathbf{A}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{O}_{2}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}
.

The proposed methodology in Sections
3.1
-
3.3
is applied to the generated data.
The ridge-type ratio method with
\bar{r}=10
and
s(p,T)=\sqrt{p\log(T)/(10T)}
is used to select
r
, and BIC in (
3.6
) is used to select
d
.
Table
1
lists the percentages of correct rank and common dimension selection.
It can be seen that both can be correctly selected almost for all cases, and the percentages of correct selection in rank and common dimension both increase as the sample size
T
increases.
This confirms the selection consistency derived in Section
4.3
.

We next compare the estimation efficiency between two models: the proposed model with a common subspace (CS) in (
2.7
) and the reduced-rank (RR) model in (
2.1
).
The median of estimation errors,
\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}
, over 500 replications is presented in Figure
1
, and the 0.75- and 0.25-th quantiles are also given in terms of error bars.
The two models have similar performances when
d=0
. However, when
d\geq 1
, the proposed model is more efficient, and the efficiency gain increases as the common dimension
d
becomes larger. This result shows that it pays to explore
the common subspace between response and predictor spaces.

The data generating process of the second experiment is a VAR(
\ell
) model in (
2.9
) with
\ell=5
, and its parameter tensor is in the form of (
2.12
) with ranks
r_{1}=r_{2}=r_{3}=3
and common dimension
0\leq d\leq 3
.
The dimension is
p=30
or 50, and we consider
T\in\{500,600,700,800\}
for
p=30
and
T\in\{1000,1200,1400,1600\}
for
p=50
.
We generate the orthonormal matrices,
\mathbf{C}
,
\mathbf{R}
,
\mathbf{P}
,
\mathbf{L}
,
\mathbf{O}_{1}
,
\mathbf{O}_{2}
and
\mathbf{O}_{3}
, randomly for each replication.
Let
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3\times 3}
be a super-diagonal tensor with diagonal entries
\{c_{1},c_{2},c_{3}\}
, and
c_{i}
’s are generated by
the same method as that of the first experiment.
The parameter tensor has the form
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{1}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\times_{2}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\times_{3}\mathbf{L}
, where
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{S}$}\times_{1}\mathbf{O}_{1}\times_{2}\mathbf{O}_{2}\times_{3}\mathbf{O}_{3}
.
The proposed methodology in Section
3.4
and Appendix
D.2
is applied to each generated sample.

Table
2
gives the percentages of correct rank and common dimension selection, respectively, and Figure
2
presents the median, 0.75- and 0.25-th quantiles of estimation errors,
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}
, from our model (CS) in (
2.12
) and the reduced-rank model (RR) in
Wang et al., 2022
.
From Table
2
, the percentages of correct rank selection are obviously smaller than those of correct common dimension selection.
This is mainly due to the fact that, when tensor ranks are over-selected, the proposed method may still correctly select the common dimension.
All other findings are similar to those of the first experiment.

7.2
VAR with diverging eigenvalue effect

Next, we conduct a simulation experiment to investigate how the diverging eigenvalues may affect the estimation procedure and support the proposed methodology in Section
5
. The DGP is the same as the first experiment with
p=40
, except for
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}=0.5\mathbf{I}_{p}+0.5\mathbf{1}_{p}\mathbf{1}_{p}^{\top}
, where
\mathbf{1}_{p}
is the
p
-dimensional vector whose entries are all one. Hence,
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
has one diverging eigenvalue as
\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})=0.5p+0.5
.

In this experiment, we apply the proposed estimation procedure with diverging eigenvalue effect, including common subspace with diverging eigenvalues (CS-DE) and reduced-rank with diverging eigenvalues (RR-DE), and compare them with the standard versions CS and RR. Table
3
contains the percentages of correct rank and common dimension selection for CS and CS-DE. When
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
has a diverging eigenvalue, the CS method fails to find the correct rank while CS-DE can consistently estimate the rank and common dimension.
The estimation errors over 500 replications are presented in Figure
3
. In all cases of
d
, the errors of RR-DE and CS-DE are significantly smaller than those of the standard methods. When
d>0
, the CS-DE performs better than RR-DE, which supports the theoretical results in Section
5
.

8
An Empirical Example

We apply the proposed methodology to a macroeconomic time series data set with
p=40
variables of the United States. The data consist of quarterly economic series from Q3-1959 to Q4-2007 with the length
T=194
.
These macroeconomic variables, selected by
Koop, 2013
, can be classified into eight categories: GDP decomposition, NAPM indices, industrial production, housing, interest rates, employment, prices, and others.
All series have been transformed to stationary series and standardized, and seasonal adjustment has also been conducted to all variables except the financial series.
More information of these macroeconomic
series can be found in Appendix
H
.

This dataset has been well studied in various factor models, and the largest eigenvalues of the sample covariance matrix
\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{y}}
is 11.68. Hence, we first apply the factor modeling procedure in
Gao and Tsay, 2022
, but no diverging eigenvalue in the white noise is found.
Following
Koop, 2013
, we consider a VAR(4) model and the parameter tensor is specified as in (
2.12
).
The modeling procedure in Section
3.4
is applied to the above high-dimensional macroeconomic time series.
The estimated ranks are
(\widehat{r}_{1},\widehat{r}_{2},\widehat{r}_{3})=(4,3,2)
, while the selected common dimension is
\widehat{d}=2
, i.e., there are two common, two response-specific, and one predictor-specific factors.
The singular values of
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}_{(1)}
are 8.87, 3.70, 1.15, and 0.56, and the first two singular values can explain the diverging eigenvalue in
\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{y}}
.
For the factor interpretation, since the tensor decomposition in (
2.12
) is not unique, we standardize the matrices
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}
,
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{P}}
to be orthonormal, and calculate their projection matrices
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}
,
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{P}}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}
, which are uniquely defined and can be used to represent the subspaces
\mathcal{M}(\widehat{\mathbf{C}})
,
\mathcal{M}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}})
, and
\mathcal{M}(\widehat{\mathbf{P}})
, respectively; see Section
2
for more details.

Figure
4
plots the calculated projection matrices
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}
,
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{P}}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}
.
It can be seen that both
\widehat{\mathbf{P}}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}
are highly sparse, and these nonzero entries exhibit certain clustering pattern, which is consistent with the classification of macroeconomic variables.
Specifically, almost all significant entries in the projection matrix of predictor-specific factors can be observed for the first two categories of variables, GDP decomposition and NAPM indices, while those of common factors are from NAMP indices, industrial production, and housing.
We may argue that the three fitted predictor factors mainly extract information from four classes of variables, including GDP, NAPM indices, industrial production and housing, for the sake of predicting the future values of all series.
The predictability of these variables is consistent with our empirical experience: GDP is the most important measure of the current status of an economy, and purchasing manager indices, industrial production indices, and housing starts are widely recognized as leading indicators of economic activities.
Nevertheless, the estimated response-specific projection matrix is much denser, indicating that almost all economic variables are related to the response-specific factors. The patterns in these projection matrices can also help us interpret the diverging eigenvalues in
\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{y}}
. Since the response-specific loading
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\in\mathbb{R}^{40\times 2}
is pervasive, strong cross-sectional dependency may exist in the conditional expectation of the response, but the common and predictor-specific factors are only related to a small subset of variables. As discussed in Remark
4
, the pervasive response loadings and sparse predictor loadings may lead to the large singular values of
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}_{(1)}
.

On the other hand, it is interesting to observe that the upper left corner of the common projection matrix is almost sparse, while those in predictor-specific and response-specific projection matrices are not. We may argue that the information of GDP extracted for predictors and responses are different in general. The GDP components in the responses are positively correlated, as shown in the green upper-left block in the response-specific projection matrix, whereas in the predictor-specific projection matrix, the first variable, real GDP, is negatively correlated to real personal consumption, private domestic investment, real exports, and government consumption and investment. By definition, as the real GDP is the summation of its decomposition, the negative relationship in the predictor subspace partially cancels out the double counting.

Finally, we compare the proposed model with two other commonly used models, the rank-constrained VAR(4) model (VAR-RR) in
Wang et al., 2022
with ranks
(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})=(4,3,2)
and the dynamic factor model in
Lam and Yao, 2012
with a low-dimensional VAR(4) for factors (DFM-VAR), in terms of rolling forecast.
Specifically, from the time point of Q1-2000 (
t=163
) to Q2-2007 (
t=192
), we fit these three models utilizing all available historical data until time
t-1
and obtain one-, two- and three-step-ahead forecasts.
We consider two forecasting tasks: one is to predict all forty variables, and the other is to only forecast the 34th series, CPI for all items, as the inflation rate is one of the typical macroeconomic variables of interest in forecasting.
The average rolling forecast errors for both tasks are summarized in Table
4
, and it can be seen that our model has the smallest errors in both tasks, especially the overall forecasting. This is due to the fact that, compared to the dynamic factor modeling, our model is able to flexibly extract useful information for responses and predictors. In the meanwhile, in the proposed model, substantial dimension reduction can be further achieved by exploring the possible common subspace between response and predictor factor spaces of the rank-constrained model. The CPI forecasting errors of both methods are quite close, possibly because CPI is only involved in response-specific factor loading and DFM can also estimate it consistently.

9
Conclusion and Discussion

Vector autoregressive and factor models are two mainstream modeling frameworks for high-dimensional time series, and they have their own strengths in real applications.
This article proposed a new model by focusing on the dependent factor structure of the series, and it was shown by simulation experiments and an empirical example that the proposed
model enjoys advantages over both VAR and factor models. Theoretical justifications are established for both computational and statistical convergence of the
new model.

The research of this article can be extended in two directions.
Firstly, heavy-tailed distributions and outliers are commonly observed in empirical data sets, which violates Assumption
2
. Robust estimation methods against the heavy-tailed distribution for high-dimensional VAR models have been investigated recently
(
Wang and Tsay, 2023
)
, and it is of practical importance to investigate the robust methods for the proposed model.
Secondly, inspired by the emerging literature on matrix and tensor-valued time series
(
Chen et al., 2021
;
Chen et al., 2022
;
Wang et al., 2021
)
, we may generalize the proposed model, methodology, and theory to autoregressive models for matrix and tensor-valued time series.

lemmasectiondefinitionsection

Appendix A
Computational convergence analysis of gradient descent

In this appendix, we present the proof of Theorem
1
and necessary lemmas for the deterministic convergence analysis.

A.1
Proof of Theorem
1

Proof.

The proof consists of five steps. In the first step, we introduce some notations and conditions essential to the convergence analysis. In the second to fourth steps, we provide a deterministic convergence result for the iterates, given that some regulatory conditions are satisfied. Finally, in the last step, we show that these regulatory conditions hold iteratively.

Step 1.
(Notations and conditions)

We begin by introducing some notations and conditions for the convergence analysis.
Denote the empirical least squares loss function as

\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A})=\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}.

(A.1)

As the matrix decomposition is not unique, for the iterate at the step
i
, define the combined estimation errors of
(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{R},\mathbf{P},\mathbf{D})
up to the optimal rotations as

\begin{split}E^{(i)}=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{O}_{c}\in\mathbb{O}^{d\times d}\\
\mathbf{O}_{r},\mathbf{O}_{p}\in\mathbb{O}^{(r-d)\times(r-d)}\end{subarray}}&\Big\{\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}^{(i)}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{p}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+\left\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}-\text{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{r})^{\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\text{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{p})\right\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\Big\}\end{split}

(A.2)

and the corresponding optimal rotations as
(\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)},\mathbf{O}_{p}^{(i)})
. For simplicity in presentation, denote
\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}=\text{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)})
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}=\text{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},\mathbf{O}_{p}^{(i)})
.

By Definition
1
, for the given sample size
T
,
\mathcal{L}
is restricted strongly convex (RSC) with parameter
\alpha
and restricted strongly smooth (RSS) with parameter
\beta
, such that for any rank-
r
matrices
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p^{\prime}}
,

\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\leq\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A})-\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime})-\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime}),\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\rangle\leq\frac{\beta}{2}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}.

(A.3)

The
\alpha
-RSC condition implies that

\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A})\geq\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime})+\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime}),\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\rangle+\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

(A.4)

and as in
Nesterov, 2003
, the convexity and
\beta
-RSS condition jointly imply that

\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime})-\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A})\geq\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}),\mathbf{A}^{\prime}-\mathbf{A}\rangle+\frac{1}{2\beta}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(A.5)

Combining these two inequalities, we have that

\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime}),\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\rangle\geq\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\beta}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime})\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

(A.6)

which is also known as the restricted correlated gradient condition in
Han et al., 2021
. Moreover, by definition, we immediately have that
\alpha\leq\beta
.

In addition, by Definition
2
, we assume that given the sample,

\xi(r,d)=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}},\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r},\\
\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\textup{F}}=1\end{subarray}}\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}}\mathbf{D}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}^{\top}\right\rangle.

(A.7)

For simplicity, we assume
b=\sigma_{1}^{1/3}
and
a=C\alpha\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\kappa^{-2}
, and the proof can readily be extended to the case with
b\asymp\sigma_{1}^{1/3}
. Before starting the proof, we also assume that the following conditions hold and will verify them in the last step. For any
i=0,1,2,\dots
, we assume that

\begin{split}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1.1b,~~\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1.1b,~~\textup{and}~~\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\leq\frac{1.1\sigma_{1}}{b^{2}},\end{split}

(A.8)

which obviously implies that

\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1.1b,~~\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1.1b,~~\text{and}~~\|\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1.1b.

(A.9)

Note that the constant 1.1 can be replaced by any arbitrary constant greater than 1. In addition, we assume that for any
i=0,1,2,\dots
,
E^{(i)}\leq C\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2}
.

Step 2.
(Upper bound of
E^{(i+1)}-E^{(i)}
)

By definition,

\begin{split}&E^{(i+1)}\\
=&\|\mathbf{R}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i+1)}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{p}^{(i+1)}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{C}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i+1)}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\\
+&\|\mathbf{D}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i+1)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i+1)}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&\|\mathbf{R}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{p}^{(i)}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{C}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\\
+&\|\mathbf{D}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(A.10)

Step 2.1.
(
\mathbf{R}
and
\mathbf{P}
steps)

By definition,
\mathbf{R}^{(i+1)}=\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\eta\nabla_{\mathbf{R}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}-\eta a[\mathbf{R}^{(i)}(\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d})+\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}]
. Thus, we have

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{R}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&\Big\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)}-\eta\Big(\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\\
&+a\mathbf{R}^{(i)}(\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d})+a\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\Big)\Big\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
+&\eta^{2}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}+a\mathbf{R}^{(i)}(\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d})+a\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
-&2\eta\left\langle\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\right\rangle\\
-&2a\eta\left\langle\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)},\mathbf{R}^{(i)}(\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d})\right\rangle\\
-&2a\eta\left\langle\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)},\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\right\rangle.\end{split}

(A.11)

First, for the second term in the right hand side of (
A.11
), by Cauchy’s inequality,

\begin{split}&\left\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}+a\mathbf{R}^{(i)}(\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d})+a\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\right\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&3\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+3a^{2}\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)}(\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
+&3a^{2}\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},\end{split}

(A.12)

where the first term can be bounded by mean inequality

\begin{split}&\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&2\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
+&2\|[\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})]\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(A.13)

By the duality of the Frobenius norm, we have

\begin{split}&\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}\\
=&\sup_{\mathbf{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times(r-d)},\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\text{F}}=1}\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top},\mathbf{W}\rangle\\
=&\sup_{\mathbf{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times(r-d)},\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\text{F}}=1}\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\mathbf{W}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\rangle\\
=&\sup_{\mathbf{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times(r-d)},\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\text{F}}=1}\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{W}\textbf{]}\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}&\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\end{bmatrix}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\right\rangle\\
\leq&\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\textup{op}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\textup{op}}\cdot\xi(r,d)\end{split}

(A.14)

and the first term can be bounded as

\begin{split}&\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&2\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}^{(i)}_{21}~\mathbf{D}^{(i)}_{22}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\xi^{2}(T,\delta)\\
+&2\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}^{(i)}_{21}~\mathbf{D}^{(i)}_{22}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&4b^{-2}\sigma_{1}^{2}\left[\xi^{2}(r,d)+\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right],\end{split}

(A.15)

the second term can be bounded as

\begin{split}&a^{2}\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)}(\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq a^{2}\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&2a^{2}b^{2}\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},\end{split}

(A.16)

and the third term can be bounded as

a^{2}\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq 2a^{2}b^{2}\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(A.17)

Thus, we have

\begin{split}&\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}+a\mathbf{R}^{(i)}(\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d})+2a\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&12b^{-2}\sigma_{1}^{2}\left[\xi^{2}(r,d)+\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right]\\
+&6a^{2}b^{2}[\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}]:=Q_{\text{R},2}.\end{split}

(A.18)

For the third term in (
A.11
), denote

\begin{split}&\left\langle\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\right\rangle\\
=&\left\langle\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\right\rangle\\
=&\left\langle\mathbf{A}_{\text{R}}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\right\rangle,\end{split}

(A.19)

where
\mathbf{A}_{\text{R}}^{(i)}=(\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{22}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}
.

For the fourth and fifth terms in (
A.11
), denote

\begin{split}T_{\text{R}}&=\langle\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)},\mathbf{R}^{(i)}(\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d})+\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\rangle\end{split}

(A.20)

Therefore, we can rewrite the last three terms in (
A.11
) as

\begin{split}&\left\langle\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}^{(i)}_{21}~\mathbf{D}^{(i)}_{22}\textbf{]}\right\rangle\\
+&a\left\langle\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)},\mathbf{R}^{(i)}(\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d})\right\rangle\\
+&a\langle\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)},\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\rangle\\
=&\left\langle\mathbf{A}_{\text{R}}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\right\rangle+aT_{\text{R}}:=Q_{\text{R},1}.\end{split}

(A.21)

Combining the bounds for the terms in (
A.11
), we have

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{R}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq-2\eta Q_{\text{R},1}+\eta^{2}Q_{\text{R},2}.\end{split}

(A.22)

Similarly, for
\mathbf{P}^{(i+1)}
, we can define similar
quantities
Q_{\text{V},1}
and
Q_{\text{V},2}
, and show that

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{P}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{p}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\|\mathbf{P}^{(i)}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{p}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq-2\eta Q_{\text{P},1}+\eta^{2}Q_{\text{P},2}.\end{split}

(A.23)

Step 2.2.
(
\mathbf{C}
step)

For
\mathbf{C}^{(i+1)}
, note that

\mathbf{C}^{(i+1)}=\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\eta\nabla_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}-\eta a[2\mathbf{C}^{(i)}(\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d})+\mathbf{U}^{(i)}\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}+\mathbf{V}^{(i)}\mathbf{V}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}].

(A.24)

Thus, we have

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{C}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&\Big\|\mathbf{C}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)}-\eta\Big\{\nabla_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}+2a\mathbf{C}^{(i)}(\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d})+a\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\\
&+a\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\Big\}\Big\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
+&\eta^{2}\Big\|\nabla_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}+2a\mathbf{C}^{(i)}(\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d})+a\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}+a\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\Big\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
-&2\eta\left\langle\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},\nabla_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}\right\rangle\\
-&2a\eta\left\langle\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},2\mathbf{C}^{(i)}(\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d})\right\rangle\\
-&2a\eta\left\langle\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}+\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\right\rangle,\end{split}

(A.25)

where

\begin{split}\nabla_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}=&\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{11}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{12}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}+\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{11}^{\top}~\mathbf{D}_{21}^{\top}\textbf{]}^{\top}.\end{split}

(A.26)

First, we have

\begin{split}&\Big\|\nabla_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}+2a\mathbf{C}^{(i)}(\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d})+a\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}+a\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\Big\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&4\|\nabla_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+16a^{2}\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)}(\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
+&4a^{2}\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+4a^{2}\|\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},\end{split}

(A.27)

where the first term can be bounded as

\begin{split}&\|\nabla_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&2\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{11}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{12}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+2\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{11}^{\top}~\mathbf{D}_{21}^{\top}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&4\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{11}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{12}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+4\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{11}^{\top}~\mathbf{D}_{21}^{\top}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
+&4\|[\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})]\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{11}^{(i)}~\mathbf{D}_{12}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
+&4\|[\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})]^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\mathbf{D}_{11}^{\top}~\mathbf{D}_{21}^{\top}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&8b^{-2}\sigma_{1}^{2}[\xi^{2}(r,d)+\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}]\end{split}

(A.28)

and the other three terms can be bounded as

a^{2}\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)}(\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq 2a^{2}b^{2}\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

(A.29)

a^{2}\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq 2a^{2}b^{2}\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

(A.30)

a^{2}\|\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq 2a^{2}b^{2}\|\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(A.31)

Thus, the second term in (
A.25
) can be bounded as

\begin{split}&\Big\|\nabla_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{L}+2a\mathbf{C}^{(i)}(\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d})+2a\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}+2a\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\Big\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&32b^{-2}\sigma_{1}^{2}[\xi^{2}(r,d)+\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}]\\
+&8a^{2}b^{2}(4\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}):=Q_{\text{C},2}.\end{split}

(A.32)

For the third term in (
A.25
),

\begin{split}&\left\langle\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},\nabla_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{L}\right\rangle\\
=&\left\langle\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\mathbf{D}_{12}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}-\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\mathbf{D}_{12}^{(i)}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{*\top},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\right\rangle\\
+&\left\langle\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)}\mathbf{D}_{21}^{(i)}\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\right\rangle\\
+&\left\langle\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{D}_{11}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)}\mathbf{D}_{11}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\right\rangle\\
+&\left\langle\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{D}_{11}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}-\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{D}_{11}^{(i)}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{*\top},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\right\rangle\\
:=&\left\langle\mathbf{A}_{\text{C}}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\right\rangle.\end{split}

(A.33)

For the fourth and fifth terms in (
A.25
), denote

\langle\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},2\mathbf{C}^{(i)}(\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d})+\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}+\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\rangle:=T_{\text{C}}.

(A.34)

Hence, we can bound the last three terms in (
A.25
) as

\begin{split}&\eta\left\langle\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},\nabla_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{L}\right\rangle+a\eta\left\langle\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},\mathbf{C}^{(i)}(\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d})\right\rangle\\
+&a\eta\left\langle\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}+\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\right\rangle\\
\geq&\left\langle\mathbf{A}_{\text{C}}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\right\rangle+aT_{\text{C}}:=Q_{\text{C},1}.\end{split}

(A.35)

Combining these bounds, we have

\|\mathbf{C}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq-2\eta Q_{\text{C},1}+\eta^{2}Q_{\text{C},2}.

(A.36)

Step 2.3.
(
\mathbf{D}
step)

For
\mathbf{D}^{(i)}
, we consider the following decomposition

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{D}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}-\eta\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\eta^{2}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
-&2\eta\left\langle\mathbf{D}^{(i)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)},\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\right\rangle.\end{split}

(A.37)

For the third term, we have

\begin{split}&\langle\mathbf{D}^{(i)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)},\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\rangle\\
=&\langle\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\rangle\\
=&\langle\mathbf{A}_{\text{D}}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\rangle:=Q_{\text{D},1}\end{split}

(A.38)

In addition,

\begin{split}&\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&2\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
+&2\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}[\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})]\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&2\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot[\xi^{2}(T,\delta)+\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}]\\
=&4b^{2}[\xi^{2}(r,d)+\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}]:=Q_{\text{D},2}.\end{split}

(A.39)

Hence, we have

\|\mathbf{D}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq-2\eta Q_{\text{D},1}+\eta^{2}Q_{\text{D},2}.

(A.40)

Together, we have that

\begin{split}E^{(i+1)}&\leq\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-2\eta Q_{\text{R},1}+\eta^{2}Q_{\text{R},2}\\
&+\|\mathbf{P}^{(i)}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{p}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-2\eta Q_{\text{P},1}+\eta^{2}Q_{\text{P},2}\\
&+\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{p}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-2\eta Q_{\text{C},1}+\eta^{2}Q_{\text{C},2}\\
&+\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-2\eta Q_{\text{D},1}+\eta^{2}Q_{\text{D},2}\\
&\leq E^{(i)}-2\eta(Q_{\text{D},1}+Q_{\text{R},1}+Q_{\text{P},1}+Q_{\text{C},1})+\eta^{2}(Q_{\text{D},2}+Q_{\text{R},2}+Q_{\text{P},2}+Q_{\text{C},2}).\end{split}

(A.41)

Step 3.
(Lower bound of
Q_{\textup{D},1}+Q_{\textup{R},1}+Q_{\textup{P},1}+Q_{\textup{C},1}
)

In the third step, we develop a lower bound for
Q_{\text{D},1}+Q_{\text{R},1}+Q_{\text{P},1}+Q_{\text{C},1}
. By definition,

\begin{split}&Q_{\text{D},1}+Q_{\text{R},1}+Q_{\text{P},1}+Q_{\text{C},1}\\
=&\langle\mathbf{A}_{\text{D}}^{(i)}+\mathbf{A}_{\text{R}}^{(i)}+\mathbf{A}_{\text{P}}^{(i)}+\mathbf{A}_{\text{C}}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\rangle+a(T_{\text{R}}+T_{\text{P}}+T_{\text{C}}).\end{split}

(A.42)

Note that

\begin{split}&\mathbf{A}_{\text{D}}^{(i)}+\mathbf{A}_{\text{R}}^{(i)}+\mathbf{A}_{\text{P}}^{(i)}+\mathbf{A}_{\text{C}}^{(i)}\\
=&3\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}\\
-&\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\\
=&\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}+\mathbf{H}^{(i)},\end{split}

(A.43)

where

\begin{split}\mathbf{H}^{(i)}=&\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\\
+&\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}\\
+&\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}^{*}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}.\end{split}

(A.44)

By Lemma
1
, since
\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1.01b
,
\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1.01b
,
\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1.01\sigma_{1}b^{-2}
,
b=\sigma_{1}^{1/3}
and
E^{(i)}\leq\sigma_{1}^{2/3}
, we can derive an upper bound for
\mathbf{H}^{(i)}
,

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{H}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}&\leq 1.01\sigma_{1}b^{-2}E^{(i)}+2(1.01b)E^{(i)}+(E^{(i)})^{3/2}\\
&\leq(4\sigma_{1}^{1/3}+\sqrt{E^{(i)}})E^{(i)}\leq 5\sigma_{1}^{1/3}E^{(i)}.\end{split}

(A.45)

By the
\alpha
-RSC and
\beta
-RSS conditions, the first term on the right hand side of (
A.42
) can be bounded as

\begin{split}&\langle\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}+\mathbf{H}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\rangle\\
=&\langle\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}+\mathbf{H}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\rangle+\langle\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\rangle\\
+&\langle\mathbf{H}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\rangle\\
\geq&\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\beta}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\|\mathbf{H}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}\\
-&|\langle\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}+\mathbf{H}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\rangle|.\end{split}

(A.46)

In addition, we have that for any
c_{1}>0

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{H}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&\frac{1}{4\beta}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\beta\|\mathbf{H}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&\frac{1}{4\beta}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\beta\left(25\sigma_{1}^{2/3}E^{(i)}\right)E^{(i)}\\
\leq&\frac{\beta}{2}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{C\alpha\sigma_{1}^{4/3}}{\kappa^{2}}E^{(i)},\end{split}

(A.47)

and

\begin{split}&|\langle\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}+\mathbf{H}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\rangle|\\
\leq&|\langle\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\rangle|\\
+&|\langle\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\rangle|\\
+&|\langle\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\rangle|\\
\leq&\xi(r,d)\left(\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}\right)\\
+&\xi(r,d)\left(\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}\right)\\
+&\xi(r,d)\left(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}\right)\\
\leq&\left[1.01^{2}b^{2}+2\times 1.01^{2}\sigma_{1}/b\right]\xi(r,d)(2E^{(i)})^{1/2}\\
\leq&4\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\xi(r,d)(2E^{(i)})^{1/2}\\
\leq&32c_{2}\sigma_{1}^{4/3}E^{(i)}+\frac{1}{4c_{2}}\xi^{2}(r,d),\end{split}

(A.48)

for any
c_{2}>0
. Combining these inequalities, we have

\begin{split}&\langle\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}+\mathbf{H}^{(i)},\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})\rangle\\
\geq&\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4\beta}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4c_{2}}\xi^{2}(r,d)\\
-&\left(32c_{2}\sigma_{1}^{4/3}+\frac{C\alpha\sigma_{1}^{4/3}}{\kappa^{2}}\right)E^{(i)}.\end{split}

(A.49)

Applying Lemma
2
with
b=\sigma_{1}^{1/3}
, we can obtain an upper bound for
E^{(i)}
,

\begin{split}&E^{(i)}\leq(4\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}+136\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\sigma_{r}^{-2})\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
+&28b^{-2}\left(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\\
\leq&140\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\sigma_{r}^{-2}\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
+&28\sigma_{1}^{-2/3}\left(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\\
\leq&140\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}\kappa^{2}\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
+&28\sigma_{1}^{-2/3}\left(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right).\end{split}

(A.50)

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
A.42
), note that

\begin{split}&T_{\text{R}}+T_{\text{P}}+T_{\text{C}}\\
=&\langle\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)},\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r})\rangle\\
+&\langle\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)},\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r})\rangle.\end{split}

(A.51)

Denote
\mathbf{U}^{(i)}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}
and
\mathbf{U}^{*}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}
. Note that

\begin{split}&\left\langle\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)},\mathbf{U}^{(i)}(\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r})\right\rangle\\
=&\left\langle\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)},\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\rangle\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{*\top}\mathbf{U}^{*},\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\rangle\\
+&\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\mathbf{U}^{*\top}\mathbf{U}^{*}-2\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}+\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)},\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\rangle\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\left(\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}\right),\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\rangle\\
+&\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\mathbf{U}^{*\top}\mathbf{U}^{*}-\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)},\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\rangle.\end{split}

(A.52)

In addition, by the fact that
\mathbf{U}^{*\top}\mathbf{U}^{*}=b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}
and
\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
, we have

\begin{split}&\left\langle\mathbf{U}^{*\top}\mathbf{U}^{*}-\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)},\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\rangle\\
=&\left\langle\mathbf{U}^{*\top}\mathbf{U}^{*}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{*\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)},\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\rangle\\
=&\left\langle\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{*\top}\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{*\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)},\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\rangle\\
=&\left\langle(\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)})^{\top}(\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{(i)}),\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\rangle\end{split}

(A.53)

and

\begin{split}&\left\langle\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)},\mathbf{U}^{(i)}(\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r})\right\rangle\\
=&\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
+&\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\left(\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{(i)}\right)^{\top}\left(\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{(i)}\right),\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\right\rangle\\
\geq&\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\geq&\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\|\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{4}-\frac{1}{4}\|\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r-d}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\geq&\frac{1}{4}\|\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}E^{(i)}\|\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}-\mathbf{U}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(A.54)

Therefore, we have

\begin{split}&T_{\text{R}}+T_{\text{P}}+T_{\text{C}}\\
\geq&\frac{1}{4}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
-&\frac{1}{4}E^{(i)}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4}E^{(i)}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(A.55)

Combining these inequalities, and since
b=\sigma_{1}^{1/3}
,

\begin{split}&Q_{\text{D},1}+Q_{\text{R},1}+Q_{\text{P},1}+Q_{\text{C},1}\\
\geq&\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4\beta}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4c_{2}}\xi^{2}(r,d)\\
-&\left[32c_{2}\sigma_{1}^{4/3}+\frac{C\alpha\sigma_{1}^{4/3}}{\kappa^{2}}\right]E^{(i)}\\
+&\frac{1}{4}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
-&\frac{a}{4}E^{(i)}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\frac{a}{4}E^{(i)}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\geq&\frac{\alpha}{2}\Bigg[\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{1}^{2/3}}{5\kappa^{2}}\left(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right.\\
+&\left.\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\Bigg]\\
+&\frac{1}{4\beta}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4c_{2}}\xi^{2}(r,d)\\
-&\left(32c_{2}\sigma_{1}^{4/3}+\frac{C\alpha\sigma_{1}^{4/3}}{\kappa^{2}}\right)E^{(i)}\\
+&\left(\frac{a}{4}-\frac{\alpha\sigma_{1}^{2/3}}{10\kappa^{2}}\right)\left(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}).\right.\end{split}

(A.56)

Letting
a=0.8\alpha\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\kappa^{-2}
and since
E^{(i)}\leq\sigma_{1}^{2/3}

\begin{split}&Q_{\text{D},1}+Q_{\text{R},1}+Q_{\text{P},1}+Q_{\text{C},1}\\
\geq&\left(\frac{\alpha\sigma_{1}^{4/3}}{140\kappa^{2}}-32c_{2}\sigma_{1}^{4/3}-\frac{C\alpha\sigma_{1}^{4/3}}{\kappa^{2}}\right)E^{(i)}\\
+&\frac{1}{4\beta}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{4c_{2}}\xi^{2}(r,d)\\
+&\frac{a}{8}\left(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right).\end{split}

(A.57)

Step 4.
(Convergence analysis of
E^{(i)}
)

In the following, we combine all the results in the previous steps to establish the error bound for
E^{(i)}
and
\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}
. Plugging in
b=\sigma_{1}^{1/3}
and
a=1.6\alpha\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\kappa^{-2}
to
Q_{\text{D},2}
,
Q_{\text{R},2}
,
Q_{\text{P},2}
and
Q_{\text{C},2}
, we have

\begin{split}&Q_{\text{D},2}+Q_{\text{R},2}+Q_{\text{P},2}+Q_{\text{C},2}\\
\leq&92\sigma_{1}^{4/3}[\xi^{2}(r,d)+\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}]\\
+&\frac{52\alpha^{2}\sigma_{1}^{2}}{\kappa^{4}}\left(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right).\end{split}

(A.58)

Combining the upper bound for
Q_{\text{D},2}+Q_{\text{R},2}+Q_{\text{P},2}+Q_{\text{C},2}
and the lower bound for
Q_{\text{D},1}+Q_{\text{R},1}+Q_{\text{P},1}+Q_{\text{C},1}
, we have

\begin{split}E^{(i+1)}&\leq\left(1-2\eta\left(\frac{\alpha\sigma_{1}^{4/3}}{140\kappa^{2}}-32c_{2}\sigma_{1}^{4/3}-\frac{C\alpha\sigma_{1}^{4/3}}{\kappa^{2}}\right)\right)E^{(i)}\\
&+\left(92\sigma_{1}^{4/3}\eta^{2}-\frac{\eta}{2\beta}\right)\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{(i)})-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+\left(\frac{\eta}{2c_{2}}+92\sigma_{1}^{4/3}\eta^{2}\right)\xi^{2}(r,d)\\
&-\left(\frac{\eta\alpha\sigma_{1}^{2/3}}{5\kappa^{2}}-\frac{52\eta^{2}\alpha^{2}\sigma_{1}^{2}}{\kappa^{4}}\right)(\|\mathbf{U}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{U}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{V}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{V}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}).\end{split}

(A.59)

Letting
c_{2}=C\alpha\kappa^{-2}
,
\eta=\eta_{0}\beta^{-1}\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}
with
\eta_{0}\leq 1/260
, since
E^{(i)}\leq C\sigma_{1}^{2/3}
, the coefficients of the second, third, and fourth term in (
A.59
) are

92\sigma_{1}^{4/3}\eta^{2}-0.5\eta\beta^{-1}=(92\eta_{0}-0.5)\eta_{0}\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}\beta^{-2}\leq 0,

(A.60)

\frac{\eta}{2c_{2}}+92\sigma_{1}^{4/3}\eta^{2}\leq\eta_{0}\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}\kappa^{2}+92\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}\beta^{-2}\leq C\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}\kappa^{2},

(A.61)

and

\frac{\eta\alpha\sigma_{1}^{2/3}}{5\kappa^{2}}-\frac{52\eta^{2}\alpha^{2}\sigma_{1}^{2}}{\kappa^{4}}=0.2\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\sigma_{1}^{-2/3}\kappa^{-2}(1-260\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})\geq 0,

(A.62)

as
\alpha\beta^{-1}\leq 1
,
\kappa^{-2}\leq 1
and
\eta_{0}\leq 1/260
.
Therefore, we can derive the following recursive inequality

\begin{split}&E^{(i+1)}\leq\left(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2}\right)E^{(i)}+C\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}\xi^{2}(r,d).\end{split}

(A.63)

By induction, we have that for any
i=1,2,\dots
,

E^{(i)}\leq(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{i}E^{(0)}+C\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}\xi^{2}(r,d).

(A.64)

For the error bound of
\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}
, by Lemma
2
,

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq C\sigma_{1}^{4/3}E^{(i)}\\
\leq&C\sigma_{1}^{4/3}(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{i}E^{(0)}+C\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}(r,d)\\
\leq&C\kappa^{2}(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{i}\|\mathbf{A}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+C\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}(r,d).\end{split}

(A.65)

Step 5.
(Verification of conditions)

Finally, we show that conditions
E^{(i)}\leq C\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2}
and (
A.8
) hold.

Since
\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(0)}~\mathbf{R}^{(0)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(0)}~\mathbf{R}^{(0)}\textbf{]}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
and
\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(0)}~\mathbf{P}^{(0)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(0)}~\mathbf{P}^{(0)}\textbf{]}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
, by Lemma
2
and initialization bound
\|\mathbf{A}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\leq C\sigma_{r}\alpha^{1/2}\beta^{-1/2}
, we have

E^{(0)}\leq(C\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}+C\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\sigma_{r}^{-2})\|\mathbf{A}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq C\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}\kappa^{2}\|\mathbf{A}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq C\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2}.

(A.66)

Based on the recursive relationship in (
A.63
), by induction it is easy to check that
E^{(i)}\leq C\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2}
for all
i\geq 1
. In other words, as
\alpha\beta^{-1}\leq 1
and
\kappa^{-2}\leq 1
, we have
E^{(i)}\leq Cb^{2}
for all
i\geq 1
, which further implies that

\begin{split}&\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\leq\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\\
\leq&b+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}\leq(1+\sqrt{C})b\leq(1+c_{b})b,\end{split}

(A.67)

and

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\leq\|\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}+\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\\
\leq&\sigma_{1}b^{-2}+\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}\|_{\text{F}}\leq(1+c_{b})\sigma_{1}b^{-2},\end{split}

(A.68)

which completes the deterministic analysis.

∎

A.2
Auxiliary lemmas

The first lemma follows from Lemma E.3 in
Han et al., 2021
with the tensor order changed from 3 to 2.

Lemma 1
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{A}^{*}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}^{*}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}
,
\mathbf{A}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}
with
\mathbf{D}^{*},\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
,
\mathbf{C}^{*},\mathbf{C}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times d}
,
\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}^{*},\mathbf{P},\mathbf{P}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times(r-d)}
,
\mathbf{O}_{c}\in\mathbb{O}^{d\times d}
,
\mathbf{O}_{r},\mathbf{O}_{p}\in\mathbb{O}^{(r-d)\times(r-d)}
,
\mathbf{O}_{1}=\textup{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{r})
, and
\mathbf{O}_{2}=\textup{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{p})
. Let

\begin{split}&\mathbf{A}_{1}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top},~~\mathbf{A}_{2}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top},\\
&\mathbf{H}_{1}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top},~~\mathbf{H}_{2}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top},\\
&\mathbf{A}_{d}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top},~~\mathbf{H}_{d}=\mathbf{D}^{*}-\mathbf{O}_{1}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}.\end{split}

(A.69)

Then, defining

\mathbf{H}=\mathbf{A}^{*}-\mathbf{A}_{d}-(\mathbf{A}_{1}-\mathbf{A})-(\mathbf{A}_{2}-\mathbf{A}),

(A.70)

we have

\|\mathbf{H}\|_{\textup{F}}\leq B_{2}B_{3}+2B_{1}B_{3}+B_{3}^{3/2},

(A.71)

where

\begin{split}B_{1}&:=\max\{\|[\mathbf{C},\mathbf{R}]\|_{\textup{op}},\|[\mathbf{C}^{*},\mathbf{R}^{*}]\|_{\textup{op}},\|[\mathbf{C},\mathbf{P}]\|_{\textup{op}},\|[\mathbf{C}^{*},\mathbf{P}^{*}]\|_{\textup{op}}\},\\
B_{2}&:=\max\{\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\textup{op}},\|\mathbf{D}^{*}\|_{\textup{op}}\},\\
B_{3}&:=\max\{\|\mathbf{H}_{d}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2},\|\mathbf{H}_{1}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2},\|\mathbf{H}_{2}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\}.\end{split}

(A.72)

Proof.

Since
\mathbf{D}^{*}=\mathbf{O}_{1}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}+\mathbf{H}_{d}
, we have

\mathbf{A}^{*}=\underbrace{\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}}_{T_{1}}+\underbrace{\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{D}}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}}_{T_{2}}.

(A.73)

For
T_{1}
, since
\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top}+\mathbf{H}_{1}
and
\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}+\mathbf{H}_{2}
, we have

\begin{split}T_{1}&=(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}+\mathbf{H}_{1}\mathbf{O}_{1})\mathbf{D}(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}+\mathbf{H}_{2}\mathbf{O}_{2})^{\top}\\
&=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}+\mathbf{H}_{1}\mathbf{O}_{1}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}+\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{\top}+\mathbf{H}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}\\
&=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C},\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}+(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]})\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}\\
&+\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]})^{\top}+\mathbf{H}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}\\
&=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}+\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}+\mathbf{H}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon},\end{split}

(A.74)

where
\mathbf{H}^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}=\mathbf{H}_{1}\mathbf{O}_{1}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{\top}
.
For
T_{2}
, we have

\begin{split}T_{2}&=(\mathbf{H}_{1}+\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top})\mathbf{H}_{d}(\mathbf{H}_{2}+\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top})^{\top}\\
&=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}]^{\top}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}+\mathbf{H}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)},\end{split}

(A.75)

where
\mathbf{H}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}=\mathbf{H}_{1}\mathbf{H}_{d}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{\top}+\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{d}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{\top}+\mathbf{H}_{1}\mathbf{H}_{d}\mathbf{O}_{2}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}
. Hence,
\mathbf{H}=\mathbf{H}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}+\mathbf{H}_{\varepsilon}^{(2)}
and

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{H}\|_{\text{F}}&\leq\|\mathbf{H}_{1}\mathbf{O}_{1}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{d}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}\\
&+\|\mathbf{H}_{1}\mathbf{H}_{d}\mathbf{O}_{2}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}+\|\mathbf{H}_{1}\mathbf{H}_{d}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}\\
&\leq B_{2}B_{3}+2B_{1}B_{3}+B_{3}^{3/2}.\end{split}

(A.76)

∎

The following lemma shares similar ideas and techniques as those of Lemma E.2 in
Han et al., 2021
with the common subspace structure included.

Lemma 2
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{A}^{*}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}^{*}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}
,
\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
,
\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
,
\sigma_{1}=\|\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{op}}
, and
\sigma_{r}=\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A}^{*})
. Let
\mathbf{A}=[\mathbf{C},\mathbf{R}]\mathbf{D}[\mathbf{C},\mathbf{P}]^{\top}
with
\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\|_{\textup{op}}\leq(1+c_{b})b
,
\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\|_{\textup{op}}\leq(1+c_{b})b
and
\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\textup{op}}\leq(1+c_{b})\sigma_{1}/b^{2}
for some constant
c_{b}>0
. Define

\begin{split}E:=&\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{O}_{c}\in\mathbb{O}^{d\times d}\\
\mathbf{O}_{r},\mathbf{O}_{p}\in\mathbb{O}^{(r-d)\times(r-d)}\end{subarray}}\big(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\textup{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{r})\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\\
&-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\textup{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{p})\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}-\textup{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{r})^{\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\textup{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{p})\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\big).\end{split}

(A.77)

Then, we have

\begin{split}E&\leq\left(4b^{-4}+\frac{8b^{2}}{\sigma_{r^{*}}^{2}}C_{b}\right)\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\\
+&2b^{-2}C_{b}\left(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\right),\end{split}

(A.78)

and

\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\leq 3b^{4}[1+4\sigma_{1}^{2}b^{-6}(1+c_{b})^{4}]E,

(A.79)

where
C_{b}=1+4\sigma_{1}^{2}b^{-6}((1+c_{b})^{4}+(1+c_{b})^{2}(2+c_{b})^{2}/2)
.

Proof.

Denote
\mathbf{O}_{1}=\textup{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{r})
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}=\textup{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{p})
.
Note that
\|\mathbf{O}_{1}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}-\mathbf{D}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}=b^{-2}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}^{*}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}
. We have the decomposition

\begin{split}&\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}^{*}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}\\
=&(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}+\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]})\mathbf{D}(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}+\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]})^{\top}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}^{*}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}\\
=&(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}^{*}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top})+(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]})\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}\\
+&\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]})^{\top}+(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]})\mathbf{D}(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]})^{\top}.\end{split}

(A.80)

By mean inequality,

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{D}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\|\mathbf{O}_{1}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}-\mathbf{D}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&4b^{-4}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+4b^{-4}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\\
+&4b^{-4}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\\
+&2b^{-4}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\\
+&2b^{-4}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\text{op}}^{2}\\
\leq&4b^{-4}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+4b^{-4}\left((1+c_{b})^{4}\sigma_{1}^{2}b^{-2}+(1+c_{b})^{2}(2+c_{b})^{2}\sigma_{1}^{2}b^{-2}/2\right)\\
&\left(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right).\end{split}

(A.81)

Hence, it follows that

\begin{split}&E\\
=&\min_{\mathbf{O}_{i},i=1,2}\left(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{D}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right)\\
\leq&4b^{-4}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+C_{b}\min_{\mathbf{O}_{i},i=1,2}\left\{\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right\},\end{split}

(A.82)

where
C_{b}=1+4\sigma^{2}b^{-6}((1+c_{b})^{4}+(1+c_{b})^{2}(2+c_{b})^{2}/2)
.

Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}
be the SVD of
\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}
. Then, we have

\begin{split}&\min_{\mathbf{O}_{1}}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&\min_{\mathbf{O}_{1}}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}-b\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}+b\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\leq&2\|\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}-b\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+2\min_{\mathbf{O}_{1}}\|b\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&2\|\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}-b\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+2\min_{\mathbf{O}_{1}}\|b\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(A.83)

Similarly to Lemma E.2 in
Han et al., 2021
, we have

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}-b\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq b^{-2}\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(A.84)

Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}
be the perpendicular orthonormal matrix of
\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}
. As
\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}
and
\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}/b
are orthonormal matrices spanning left singular subspaces of
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{A}^{*}
, we have

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}&\geq\|\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\|\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&=\|\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}/b)(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}/b)^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&\geq\sigma_{r}^{2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}/b)\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.\end{split}

(A.85)

By Lemma 1 in
Cai and Zhang, 2018
,

\min_{\mathbf{O}_{1}}\|b\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq 2b^{2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq 2b^{2}\frac{\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}}{\sigma_{r}^{2}}.

(A.86)

These imply that

\begin{split}E&\leq\left(4b^{-4}+\frac{8b^{2}}{\sigma_{1}^{2}}c_{b}\right)\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+2b^{-2}c_{b}\left(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\right).\end{split}

(A.87)

For the second inequality, denote the optimal rotation matrices by

\begin{split}(\mathbf{O}_{1},\mathbf{O}_{2})=\argmin_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Q}_{k},k=1,2\end{subarray}}&\big\{\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{Q}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{Q}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{D}-\mathbf{Q}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\mathbf{Q}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\big\}.\end{split}

(A.88)

Let
\mathbf{H}_{d}=\mathbf{D}^{*}-\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}
,
\mathbf{H}_{1}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top}
, and
\mathbf{H}_{2}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top}
. Then,

\mathbf{A}^{*}=(\mathbf{H}_{1}+\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top})(\mathbf{H}_{d}+\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2})(\mathbf{H}_{2}+\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}^{\top})^{\top}

(A.89)

and

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{A}^{*}-\mathbf{A}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{H}_{d}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}+\|\mathbf{H}_{1}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}\\
