+&\|\mathbf{H}_{1}\mathbf{O}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{O}_{2}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\mathbf{H}_{d}\|_{\text{F}}+\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\mathbf{H}_{1}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\mathbf{H}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}\\
+&\frac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\mathbf{H}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}\cdot\|\mathbf{H}_{2}\|_{\text{op}}+\|\mathbf{H}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}\cdot\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\mathbf{H}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&b^{2}\|\mathbf{H}_{d}\|_{\text{F}}+\sigma_{1}b^{-1}[(1+c_{b})^{2}+(1+c_{b})(2+c_{b})/2](\|\mathbf{H}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}+\|\mathbf{H}_{2}\|_{\text{F}})\\
\leq&b^{2}\|\mathbf{H}_{d}\|_{\text{F}}+2\sigma_{1}b^{-1}(1+c_{b})^{2}(\|\mathbf{H}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}+\|\mathbf{H}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}).\end{split}

(A.90)

Hence,

\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq 3\left[b^{4}\|\mathbf{H}_{d}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+4\sigma_{1}^{2}b^{-2}(1+c_{b})^{4}(\|\mathbf{H}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{H}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2})\right].

(A.91)

∎

Appendix B
Statistical convergence analysis of gradient descent

In this appendix, we present the stochastic properties of the time series data.

Proof of Theorem
2
.

The proof of Theorem
2
consists of two steps. In the first step, we show that the RSC, RSS and deviation bound conditions defined in the deterministic computational convergence analysis hold with high probability, and proofs of these conditions are presented in Appendix
B.1
. Given these regularity conditions, it suffices to show the statistical properties of the initial values, which will be discussed in Appendix
B.2
.

By Lemmas
3
and
4
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-2},\tau^{-4})T]-\exp(-Cp)
, the empirical loss function
\mathcal{L}(\cdot)
satisfies the RSC-
\alpha_{\text{RSC}}
and RSS-
\beta_{\text{RSS}}
conditions, and

\xi(r,d)\lesssim\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\textup{CS}}(p,r,d)}{T}}.

(B.1)

By Theorem
1
, we have that, for all
i=1,2,\dots
,

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
\lesssim&\kappa^{2}(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{i}\|\mathbf{A}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\xi^{2}(r,d).\end{split}

(B.2)

Hence, when

I\gtrsim\frac{\log(\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\xi^{2}(r,d))-\log(\|\mathbf{A}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2})}{\log(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})},

(B.3)

the statistical error will absorb the optimization error, so

\|\mathbf{A}^{(I)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\lesssim\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-1}\beta^{-1}\xi^{2}(r,d).

(B.4)

Moreover, by Lemma
6
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-2},\tau^{-4})T]-\exp(-Cp)
,

\|\mathbf{A}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\kappa^{2}g_{\min}^{-2}\alpha^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{d_{\text{RR}}(p,r)/T}.

(B.5)

Combining these results, we have that when

I\gtrsim\frac{\log(\kappa^{-2}\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}g_{\min}^{2}\alpha_{\text{RSC}}\beta_{\text{RSS}}^{-1}[d_{\text{CS}}(p,r,d)/d_{\text{RR}}(p,r)])}{\log(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha_{\text{RSC}}\beta_{\text{RSS}}^{-1}\kappa^{-2})},

(B.6)

with probability at least
1-4\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-2},\tau^{-4})T]-2\exp(-Cp)
,

\|\mathbf{A}^{(I)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\kappa\alpha_{\text{RSC}}^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\text{CS}}(p,r,d)}{T}}.

(B.7)

∎

B.1
Proofs of RSC, RSS and deviation bound

We first prove the restricted strong convexity (RSC) and restricted strong smoothness (RSS) conditions. For the least squares loss function
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A})={(2T)}^{-1}\|\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}
, it is easy to check that for any
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
,

\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A})-\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime})-\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime}),\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\rangle=\frac{1}{2T}\|(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime})\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime})\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}.

(B.8)

Lemma 3
.

Assume the conditions in Theorem
2
hold. Suppose that
T\gtrsim M_{2}^{-2}\max(\tau^{4},\tau^{2})p
. For any rank-
2r
matrix
\mathbf{\Delta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T]
,

\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\beta_{\textup{RSS}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2},

(B.9)

where
\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}=\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})/(2\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A}))
and
\beta_{\textup{RSS}}=(3\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}))/(2\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A}))
.

Proof of Lemma
3
.

For any
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times p}
, denote
R_{T}(\mathbf{M})=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|\mathbf{M}\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
. Note that
R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})\geq\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})-\sup_{\mathbf{\Delta}}|R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})|
.

Based on the moving average representation of VAR(1), we can rewrite
\mathbf{y}_{t}
as a VMA(
\infty
) process,

\mathbf{y}_{t}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}+\mathbf{A}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t-1}+\mathbf{A}^{2}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t-2}+\mathbf{A}^{3}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t-3}+\cdots

(B.10)

Let
\mathbf{z}=(\mathbf{y}_{T-1}^{\top},\mathbf{y}_{T-2}^{\top},\dots,\mathbf{y}_{0}^{\top})^{\top}
,
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}=(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{T-1}^{\top},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{T-2}^{\top},\dots,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{0},\dots)^{\top}
, and
\boldsymbol{\zeta}=(\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{T-1}^{\top},\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{T-2}^{\top},\dots,\boldsymbol{\zeta}_{0},\dots)^{\top}
.
Note that
\mathbf{z}=\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
, where
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}
is defined as

\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{I}_{p}&\mathbf{A}&\mathbf{A}^{2}&\mathbf{A}^{3}&\dots\\
\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{I}_{p}&\mathbf{A}&\mathbf{A}^{2}&\dots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots\\
\end{bmatrix}.

(B.11)

Then, we have

\begin{split}&R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})=\mathbf{z}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{T}\otimes\mathbf{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{\Delta})\mathbf{z}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{T}\otimes\mathbf{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{\Delta})\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\\
=&\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{T}\otimes\mathbf{\Delta}^{\top}\mathbf{\Delta})\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\boldsymbol{\zeta}:=\boldsymbol{\zeta}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma_{\mathbf{\Delta}}}\boldsymbol{\zeta},\end{split}

(B.12)

where

\widetilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{1/2}&\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\cdots\\
\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{1/2}&\mathbf{O}&\cdots\\
\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{1/2}&\cdots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots\end{bmatrix}.

(B.13)

Thus,
\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})=\|(\mathbf{I}_{T}\otimes\mathbf{\Delta})\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\geq T\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})
.

As
\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}=1
, by the sub-multiplicative property of the Frobenius norm and operator norm, we have

\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{\Delta}}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq T\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})

(B.14)

and

\|\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{\Delta}}\|_{\text{op}}\leq\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}).

(B.15)

For any
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{p-1}
and any
t>0
, by Hanson-Wright inequality,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}[|R_{T}(\mathbf{v}^{\top})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{v}^{\top})|\geq t]\\
\leq&2\exp\left(-\min\left(\frac{t^{2}}{\tau^{4}T\lambda^{2}_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda^{2}_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})},\frac{t}{\tau^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda^{2}_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})}\right)\right).\end{split}

(B.16)

Considering an
\epsilon
-covering net of
\mathbb{S}^{p-1}
, by Lemma
9
, we can easily construct the union bound for
T\gtrsim p
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{p-1}}|R_{T}(\mathbf{v}^{\top})-\mathbb{E}R_{n}(\mathbf{v}^{\top})|\geq t\right]\\
\leq&C\exp\left(p-\min\left(\frac{t^{2}}{\tau^{4}T\lambda^{2}_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda^{2}_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})},\frac{t}{\tau^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda^{2}_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})}\right)\right).\end{split}

(B.17)

Letting
t=T\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})/2
, for
T\gtrsim M_{2}^{-2}\max(\tau^{4},\tau^{2})p
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{p-1}}|R_{n}(\mathbf{v}^{\top})-\mathbb{E}R_{n}(\mathbf{v}^{\top})|\geq n\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})/2\right]\\
\leq&2\exp\left[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T\right],\end{split}

(B.18)

where
M_{2}=[\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})]/[\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})]
.

Therefore, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T]
,

R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})\geq\frac{T}{2}\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(B.19)

Similarly,
R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})\leq\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})+\sup_{\mathbf{\Delta}}|R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})|
and
\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})\leq T\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\cdot\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})
. Additionally, the upper bound in (
B.18
) can easily be expanded to
T\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})/2
, and the upper bound of
R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})
follows.

Finally, since
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}
is related to the VMA(
\infty
) process, by the spectral measure of ARMA process discussed in
Basu and Michailidis, 2015
, we may replace
\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})
and
\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})
with
1/\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})
and
1/\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A})
, respectively.

∎

We next prove the deviation bound for
\xi(r,d)
. For the least squares loss function
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A})=(2T)^{-1}\|\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}
, it is clear that

\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*})=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top}.

(B.20)

Lemma 4
.

Assume conditions in Theorem
2
hold. If
T\gtrsim M_{2}^{-2}\max(\tau^{4},\tau^{2})p
, then, with probability at least
1-\exp(-Cp)
,

\xi(r,d):=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}},\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r}\\
\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}\end{subarray}}\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}\right\rangle\lesssim\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\textup{CS}}(p,r,d)}{T}}

(B.21)

where
M_{1}=\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})/\mu_{\min}^{1/2}(\mathcal{A})
.

Proof of Lemma
4
.

Denote
\mathcal{W}(r,d;p)=\{\mathbf{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}:\mathbf{W}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top},~\mathbf{C}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times d},~\mathbf{R},\mathbf{P}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times(r-d)},~\text{and}~\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\text{F}}=1\}
. By definition,

\xi(r,d)=\sup_{\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{W}(r,d;p)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{W}\right\rangle.

(B.22)

First, we consider an
\epsilon
-net
\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)
for
\mathcal{W}(r,d;p)
. For any matrix
\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{W}(r,d;p)
, there exists a matrix
\overline{\mathbf{W}}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)
such that
\|\mathbf{W}-\overline{\mathbf{W}}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\epsilon
. Obviously,
\mathbf{\Delta}=\mathbf{W}-\overline{\mathbf{W}}
is a rank-
2r
matrix with common dimension
2d
. Based on the SVD of
\mathbf{\Delta}
, we can split the first
2r
pairs of left and right singular vectors into two equal-size groups such that the dimension of left and right singular vectors is
r-d
in each group. By the splitting of SVD, we can write
\mathbf{\Delta}=\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}+\mathbf{\Delta}_{2}
, where both
\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
and
\mathbf{\Delta}_{2}
are rank-
r
matrix with common dimension
d
and
\langle\mathbf{\Delta}_{1},\mathbf{\Delta}_{2}\rangle=0
.

By Cauchy’s inequality, as
\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}
, we have
\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}\|_{\text{F}}+\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{2}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\sqrt{2}\epsilon
. Moreover, since
\mathbf{\Delta}_{i}/\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}\in\mathcal{W}(r,d;p)
,

\begin{split}\xi(r,d)&\leq\max_{\mathbf{W}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{W}\right\rangle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{\Delta}_{i}/\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}\right\rangle\|\mathbf{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\text{F}}\\
&\leq\max_{\mathbf{W}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{W}\right\rangle+\sqrt{2}\epsilon\xi(r,d),\end{split}

(B.23)

which implies that

\xi(r,d)\leq(1-\sqrt{2}\epsilon)^{-1}\max_{\mathbf{W}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{W}\right\rangle.

(B.24)

Next, for any fixed
\mathbf{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
such that
\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\text{F}}=1
,
\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{W}\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\mathbf{W}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\rangle
, and we denote
S_{t}(\mathbf{W})=\sum_{s=1}^{t}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{s},\mathbf{W}\mathbf{y}_{s-1}\rangle
and
R_{t}(\mathbf{W})=\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\|\mathbf{W}\mathbf{y}_{s}\|_{2}^{2}
, for
1\leq t\leq T
. Similar to
Wang et al., 2021
, by the standard Chernoff bound, for any
z_{1}>0
and
z_{2}>0
,

\mathbb{P}[\{S_{T}(\mathbf{W})\geq z_{1}\}\cap\{R_{T}(\mathbf{W})\leq z_{2}\}]\leq\exp\left(-\frac{z_{1}^{2}}{2\tau^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}z_{2})}\right).

(B.25)

Similar to Lemma
3
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T]
,

R_{T}(\mathbf{W})\geq\frac{T}{2}\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top}).

(B.26)

Therefore, for any
x>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{W}(1;p)}\left\langle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{W}\right\rangle\geq x\right]\\
\leq&\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{\overline{\mathbf{W}}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}(1;p)}\left\langle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{W}\right\rangle\geq(1-\sqrt{2}\epsilon)x\right]\\
\leq&|\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)|\cdot\mathbb{P}\left[\left\langle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{W}\right\rangle\geq(1-\sqrt{2}\epsilon)x\right]\\
\leq&|\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)|\cdot\Big\{\mathbb{P}[\{S_{T}(\mathbf{W})\geq T(1-\sqrt{2}\epsilon)x\}\cap\{R_{T}(\mathbf{W})\leq C\tau^{2}T\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})\}]\\
+&\mathbb{P}[R_{T}(\mathbf{W})>C\tau^{2}T\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})]\Big\}\\
\leq&|\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)|\cdot\Bigg\{\exp\left[-\frac{CTx^{2}}{\tau^{4}\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})}\right]+2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T]\Bigg\}.\end{split}

(B.27)

By Lemma
8
,
|\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)|\leq(24/\epsilon)^{p(2r-d)+r^{2}}
. Thus, if we take
\epsilon=0.1
and
x=C\tau^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})\sqrt{[p(2r-d)+r^{2}]/T}
, when
T\gtrsim M_{2}^{-2}\max(\tau^{4},\tau^{2})p
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{W}(r,d;p)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{W}\right\rangle\gtrsim\tau^{2}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\max}^{1/2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})\sqrt{\frac{d_{\textup{CS}}(p,r,d)}{T}}\right]\leq\exp(-Cp).

(B.28)

Finally, by the spectral measure of ARMA processes, we can replace
\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})
with
1/\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})
.

∎

B.2
Properties of initial value

We present some statistical properties of the initial value of the gradient descent algorithm, the reduced-rank estimator and the corresponding
\mathbf{C}
,
\mathbf{R}
,
\mathbf{P}
, and
\mathbf{D}
. Consider the reduced-rank estimator

\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\textup{RR}}(r)=\argmin_{\textup{rank}(\mathbf{A})\leq r}\frac{1}{2T}\|\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\widehat{\mathbf{H}}^{\top}\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{X}^{-1}(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top})^{-1}

(B.29)

where
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r}
contains the leading eigenvectors of
\mathbf{Y}\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\mathbf{X}^{\top})^{-1}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{Y}^{\top}
.

Lemma 5
.

Under Assumptions
1
–
3
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-2},\tau^{-4})T]-\exp(-Cp)
,

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\textup{RR}}(r)-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}\lesssim\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\textup{RR}}(p,r)}{T}}.

(B.30)

Proof.

Denote
\mathbf{\Delta}=\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\textup{RR}}(r)-\mathbf{A}^{*}
, then by the optimality of the reduced-rank estimator

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mathbf{y}_{t}-\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}\\
\Rightarrow&\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t},\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\rangle\\
\Rightarrow&\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{\Delta}\rangle.\end{split}

(B.31)

Since the rank of both
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\textup{RR}}
and
\mathbf{A}^{*}
is
r
,
\mathbf{\Delta}
is at most rank
2r
. Denote the set of matrices
\mathcal{W}(r;p)=\{\mathbf{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}:\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\text{F}}=1,~\text{rank}(\mathbf{W})\leq r\}
. Then, we have

\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}\sup_{\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{W}(r;p)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{W}\right\rangle.

(B.32)

By Lemma
3
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T]
,

\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}}{2}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

(B.33)

and thus,

\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\frac{2}{\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}}\sup_{\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{W}(r;p)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{W}\right\rangle.

(B.34)

By Lemma
4
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-Cp)
,

\xi(r,0)=\sup_{\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{W}(r;p)}\left\langle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{W}\right\rangle\lesssim\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\textup{RR}}(p,r)}{T}}.

(B.35)

Combining these results, we have that with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T]-\exp(-Cp)
,

\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\textup{RR}}(p,r)}{T}}.

(B.36)

∎

Next, we derive the estimation error rate for the resulting initial estimator
\mathbf{C}
,
\mathbf{R}
,
\mathbf{P}
, and
\mathbf{D}
.

Lemma 6
.

Suppose that Assumptions
1
–
3
hold. If

T\gtrsim\max(\tau^{4},\tau^{2})M_{2}^{-2}p
, then

\|\mathbf{A}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}\lesssim\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\kappa^{2}g_{\min}^{-2}\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\textup{RR}}(p,r)}{T}}

(B.37)

with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-2},\tau^{-4})T]-\exp(-Cp)
.

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we assume that the true values
\mathbf{C}^{*}
,
\mathbf{R}^{*}
and
\mathbf{P}^{*}
satisfy that
\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
and
\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}=\mathbf{I}_{r}
.

We begin with the rate of
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}
. Based on Lemma
5
, we have that
\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}=o_{p}(1)
. By Lemma
11
, we have that

\|\sin\Theta(\widetilde{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{U}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\frac{\sigma_{1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}}{\sigma_{r}^{2}}\asymp\kappa\sigma_{r}^{-1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}

(B.38)

and

\|\sin\Theta(\widetilde{\mathbf{V}},\mathbf{V}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\frac{\sigma_{1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}}{\sigma_{r}^{2}}\asymp\kappa\sigma_{r}^{-1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}.

(B.39)

By triangle inequality,

\begin{split}&\|\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top})-\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{*\top}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top})\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&\|(\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{U}^{*\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top})\|_{\text{F}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{*\top}(\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top}-\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top})\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&\|\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}\|_{\text{op}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{U}^{*\top}\|_{\text{F}}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{*\top}\|_{\text{op}}\|\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top}-\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&\sqrt{2}(\|\sin\Theta(\widetilde{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{U}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}+\|\sin\Theta(\widetilde{\mathbf{V}},\mathbf{V}^{*})\|_{\text{F}})\\
\asymp&\kappa\sigma_{r}^{-1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}.\end{split}

(B.40)

By Assumption
3
,
\sigma_{r-d}(\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{*\top}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top}))=\sigma_{r-d}(\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{*\top}\mathbf{V}^{*}_{\perp})\geq g_{\min}
. By Lemma
11
,

\begin{split}\|\sin\Theta(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}},\mathbf{R}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}&\lesssim\frac{\|\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top})-\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{*}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{*\top}(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top})\|_{\text{F}}}{g_{\min}^{2}}\\
&\asymp\kappa\sigma_{r}^{-1}g_{\min}^{-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}.\end{split}

(B.41)

Similarly, we also have

\|\sin\Theta(\widetilde{\mathbf{P}},\mathbf{P}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\kappa\sigma_{r}^{-1}g_{\min}^{-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}.

(B.42)

For
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}
, by triangle inequality,

\begin{split}&\|(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{R}^{*\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{P}^{*\top})(\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{U}^{*\top}+\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{R}^{*\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{P}^{*\top})\\
&-(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})(\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&\|(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{R}^{*\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{P}^{*\top})(\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{U}^{*\top}+\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{R}^{*\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{P}^{*\top})\|_{\text{F}}\\
+&\|(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top})(\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}^{\top}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{P}^{*\top})(\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{U}^{*\top}+\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{R}^{*\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{P}^{*\top})\|_{\text{F}}\\
+&\|(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})(\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{U}^{*\top}+\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top}-\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}-\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{R}^{*\top})\\
&(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{P}^{*\top})\|_{\text{F}}\\
+&\|(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})(\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top})(\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{R}^{*\top}-\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{P}^{*\top})\|_{\text{F}}\\
+&\|(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})(\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{\top})(\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{P}^{*\top}-\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}^{\top})\|_{\text{F}}\\
\lesssim&\|\sin\Theta(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}},\mathbf{R}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}+\|\sin\Theta(\widetilde{\mathbf{P}},\mathbf{P}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}+\|\sin\Theta(\widetilde{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{U}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}+\|\sin\Theta(\widetilde{\mathbf{V}},\mathbf{V}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}\\
\lesssim&\kappa\sigma_{r}^{-1}g_{\min}^{-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}.\end{split}

(B.43)

By Lemma
10
, since

(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{R}^{*\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{P}^{*\top})(\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{U}^{*\top}+\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{R}^{*\top})(\mathbf{I}_{p}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{P}^{*\top})=2\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{C^{*\top}},

(B.44)

we have that

\|\sin\Theta(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}},\mathbf{C}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\kappa\sigma_{r}^{-1}g_{\min}^{-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}.

(B.45)

Denote

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{c}&=\argmin_{\mathbf{O}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times d}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}\mathbf{O}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{r}&=\argmin_{\mathbf{O}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times(r-d)}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{O}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{p}&=\argmin_{\mathbf{O}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times(r-d)}}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\mathbf{O}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\|_{\text{F}},\end{split}

(B.46)

and let
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{1}=\text{diag}(\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{c},\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{r})

and
\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{2}=\text{diag}(\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{c},\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{p})
.
For
\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}
and
\mathbf{D}^{*}
,

\begin{split}&\|\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{1}^{\top}\textbf{[}\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}~\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\textbf{]}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}\textbf{[}\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}~\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\textbf{]}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{2}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{*}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&\|\textbf{[}\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}~\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\textbf{]}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{1}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}\cdot\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}~\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\\
+&\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}~\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\\
+&\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}~\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\textbf{]}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{2}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\lesssim&\kappa^{2}g_{\min}^{-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}.\end{split}

(B.47)

In summary, when
b\asymp\sigma_{1}^{1/3}
, we have that

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{C}^{(0)}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{c}-b\mathbf{C}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}=b\|\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{c}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}&\lesssim\sigma_{1}^{4/3}\sigma_{r}^{-2}g_{\min}^{-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}},\\
\|\mathbf{R}^{(0)}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{r}-b\mathbf{R}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}=b\|\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{r}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}&\lesssim\sigma_{1}^{4/3}\sigma_{r}^{-2}g_{\min}^{-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}},\\
\|\mathbf{P}^{(0)}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{r}-b\mathbf{P}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}=b\|\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{p}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}&\lesssim\sigma_{1}^{4/3}\sigma_{r}^{-2}g_{\min}^{-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}},\\
\text{and}~\|\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{D}^{(0)}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{2}-b^{-2}\mathbf{D}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}=b^{-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{1}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{D}}\widetilde{\mathbf{O}}_{2}-\mathbf{D}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}&\lesssim\sigma_{1}^{4/3}\sigma_{r}^{-2}g_{\min}^{-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}.\end{split}

(B.48)

By Lemmas
2
and
5
, with probability approaching one,

\|\mathbf{A}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\kappa^{2}g_{\min}^{-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\kappa^{2}g_{\min}^{-2}\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\text{RR}}(p,r)}{T}}.

(B.49)

∎

B.3
Auxiliary lemmas

We first present a deviation bound inequality for the quadratic term
R_{T}(\mathbf{M})=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|\mathbf{M}\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
. This is Lemma 6 in
Wang et al., 2021

Lemma 7
.

For any
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
such that
\|\mathbf{M}\|_{\textup{F}}=1
and any
t>0
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}[|R_{T}(\mathbf{M})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{M})|\geq t]\\
\leq&2\exp\left(-\min\left(\frac{t^{2}}{\tau^{4}T\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})},\frac{t}{\tau^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda^{2}_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})}\right)\right),\end{split}

(B.50)

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}
is defined as

\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{I}_{p}&\mathbf{A}^{*}&\mathbf{A}^{*2}&\mathbf{A}^{*3}&\dots&\mathbf{A}^{*(T-1)}&\dots\\
\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{I}_{p}&\mathbf{A}^{*}&\mathbf{A}^{*2}&\dots&\mathbf{A}^{*(T-2)}&\dots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\dots\\
\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\dots&\mathbf{I}_{p}&\dots\end{bmatrix}.

(B.51)

The following lemma is the covering number of
\mathcal{W}(r,d;p)
. The proof essentially follows that of the Lemma 3.1 in
Candès and Plan, 2011
.

Lemma 8
.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)
be an
\epsilon
-net of
\mathcal{W}(r,d;p)
, where
\epsilon\in(0,1]
. Then

|\overline{\mathcal{W}}(r,d;p)|\leq\left(\frac{24}{\epsilon}\right)^{p(2r-d)+r^{2}}.

(B.52)

Proof.

For any
\mathbf{W}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}\in\mathcal{W}(r,d;p)
, where
\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]},\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r}
and
\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
, we construct an
\epsilon
-net for
\mathbf{W}
by covering the set of
\mathbf{C}
,
\mathbf{R}
,
\mathbf{P}
, and
\mathbf{D}
.

By Lemma
9
, we take
\overline{\mathbb{D}}
to be an
\epsilon/8
-net for
\mathbf{D}
with
|\overline{\mathbb{D}}|\leq(24/\epsilon)^{r^{2}}
.

Next, to cover
\mathbb{O}^{p\times r}
, we consider the
\|\cdot\|_{2,\infty}
norm, defined as

\|\mathbf{X}\|_{2,\infty}=\max_{i}\|\mathbf{X}_{i}\|_{2},

(B.53)

where
\mathbf{X}_{i}
is the
i
-th column of
\mathbf{X}
. Let
\mathbb{Q}^{p\times r}=\{\mathbf{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}:\|\mathbf{X}\|_{2,\infty}\leq 1\}
. It can be easily checked that
\mathbb{O}^{p\times r}\subset\mathbb{Q}^{p\times r}
, and thus an
\epsilon/8
-net
\overline{\mathbb{O}}^{p\times r}
for
\mathbb{O}^{p\times r}
obeying
|\overline{\mathbb{O}}^{p\times r}|\leq(24/\epsilon)^{pr}
.

Denote
\overline{\mathcal{W}}=\{\overline{\mathbf{D}}\in\overline{\mathbb{D}},\overline{\mathbf{C}}\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}^{p\times d},\overline{\mathbf{R}}\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}^{p\times(r-d)},\overline{\mathbf{P}}\in\overline{\mathbb{O}}^{p\times(r-d)}\}
and we have

|\overline{\mathcal{W}}|\leq|\overline{\mathbb{D}}|\times|\overline{\mathbb{O}}^{p\times d}|\times|\overline{\mathbb{O}}^{p\times(r-d)}|^{2}=\left(\frac{24}{\epsilon}\right)^{p(2r-d)+r^{2}}.

(B.54)

It suffices to show that for any
\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{W}(r,d;p)
, there exists a
\overline{\mathbf{W}}\in\overline{\mathcal{W}}
such that
\|\mathbf{W}-\overline{\mathbf{W}}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\epsilon
.

For any fixed
\mathbf{W}\in\mathcal{W}(r,d;p)
, decompose it as
\mathbf{W}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}
. Then, there exist
\overline{\mathbf{W}}=\textbf{[}\overline{\mathbf{C}}~\overline{\mathbf{R}}\textbf{]}\overline{\mathbf{D}}\textbf{[}\overline{\mathbf{C}}~\overline{\mathbf{P}}\textbf{]}^{\top}
satisfying that
\|\overline{\mathbf{C}}-\mathbf{C}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\epsilon/8
,
\|\overline{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{R}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\epsilon/8
,
\|\overline{\mathbf{P}}-\mathbf{P}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\epsilon/8
, and
\|\overline{\mathbf{D}}-\mathbf{D}\|_{\text{F}}\leq\epsilon/8
.
This gives

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{W}-\overline{\mathbf{W}}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&\|(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\overline{\mathbf{C}}~\overline{\mathbf{R}}\textbf{]})\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}+\|\textbf{[}\overline{\mathbf{C}}~\overline{\mathbf{R}}\textbf{]}(\mathbf{D}-\overline{\mathbf{D}})\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}\\
+&\|\textbf{[}\overline{\mathbf{C}}~\overline{\mathbf{R}}\textbf{]}\overline{\mathbf{D}}(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\overline{\mathbf{C}}~\overline{\mathbf{P}}\textbf{]})^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}\\
\leq&\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\text{F}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}-\overline{\mathbf{C}}~\mathbf{R}-\overline{\mathbf{R}}\textbf{]}\|_{2,\infty}\\
+&\|\textbf{[}\overline{\mathbf{C}}~\overline{\mathbf{R}}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\mathbf{D}-\overline{\mathbf{D}}\|_{\text{F}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\\
+&\|\overline{\mathbf{D}}\|_{\text{F}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\overline{\mathbf{C}}~\overline{\mathbf{R}}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\cdot\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}-\overline{\mathbf{C}}~\mathbf{P}-\overline{\mathbf{P}}\textbf{]}\|_{2,\infty}\\
\leq&\frac{\epsilon}{4}+\frac{\epsilon}{4}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}=\epsilon.\end{split}

(B.55)

∎

The next lemma is the covering number of the
p
-dimensional unit sphere, which follows directly from Corollary 4.2.13 of
Vershynin, 2018
.

Lemma 9
.

Let
\mathcal{N}
be an
\epsilon
-net of the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{p-1}
, where
\epsilon\in(0,1]
. Then,

|\mathcal{N}|\leq\left(\frac{3}{\epsilon}\right)^{p}.

(B.56)

The following two lemmas are variants of the Davis-Kahan theorem for eigenvector perturbation for symmetric matrices and singular vector perturbation for generic matrices. These results are Theorems 2 and 4 in
Yu et al., 2015
. To make the proof self-contained, they are presented below.

Lemma 10
.

Let
\mathbf{\Sigma}
,
\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
be symmetric, with eigenvalues
\lambda_{1}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{p}
and
\widehat{\lambda}_{1}\geq\dots\geq\widehat{\lambda}_{p}
, respectively. Fix
1\leq r\leq s\leq p
and assume that
\min(\lambda_{r-1}-\lambda_{r},\lambda_{s}-\lambda_{s+1})>0
, where
\lambda_{0}:=\infty
and
\lambda_{p+1}:=-\infty
. Let
d:=s-r+1
, and let
\mathbf{V}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{v}_{r}~\mathbf{v}_{r+1}~\dots~\mathbf{v}_{s}\textbf{]}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times d}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}=\textbf{[}\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{r}~\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{r+1}~\dots~\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{s}\textbf{]}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times d}
contain the eigenvectors corresponding to the eigenvalues. Then

\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\mathbf{V}},\mathbf{V})\|_{\textup{F}}\leq\frac{2\min(d^{1/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}-\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\textup{op}},\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}-\mathbf{\Sigma}\|_{\text{F}})}{\min(\lambda_{r-1}-\lambda_{r},\lambda_{s}-\lambda_{s-1})}.

(B.57)

Lemma 11
.

Let
\mathbf{A}
,
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}
have singular values
\sigma_{1}\geq\dots\geq\sigma_{\min(p,q)}
and
\widehat{\sigma}_{1}\geq\dots\geq\widehat{\sigma}_{\min(p,q)}
, respectively. Fix
1\leq r\leq s\leq\textup{rank}(\mathbf{A})
and assume that
\min(\sigma_{r-1}^{2}-\sigma_{r}^{2},\sigma_{s}^{2}-\sigma_{s-1}^{2})>0
, where
\sigma_{0}^{2}:=\infty
and
\sigma_{\textup{rank}(\mathbf{A})+1}^{2}=-\infty
. Let
d:=s-r+1
, and let
\mathbf{V}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{v}_{r}~\mathbf{v}_{r+1}~\dots~\mathbf{v}_{s}\textbf{]}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times d}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{V}}=\textbf{[}\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{r}~\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{r+1}~\dots~\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_{s}\textbf{]}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times d}
contain the right singular vectors. Then,

\|\sin\Theta(\widehat{\mathbf{V}},\mathbf{V})\|_{\textup{F}}\leq\frac{2(2\sigma_{1}+\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\|_{\textup{op}})\min(d^{1/2}\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\|_{\textup{op}},\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\|_{\textup{F}})}{\min(\sigma_{r-1}^{2}-\sigma_{r}^{2},\sigma_{s}^{2}-\sigma_{s-1}^{2})}.

(B.58)

Appendix C
Determination of rank and common dimension

We start from the proof of rank selection consistency in Theorem
3
.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

Following the proof of Lemma
5
, if
T\gtrsim\max(\tau^{4},\tau^{2})M_{2}^{-2}p
, then with probability approaching one,

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r})-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\text{RR}}(p,\bar{r})}{T}}.

(C.1)

Obviously,
\text{rank}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r})-\mathbf{A}^{*})\leq\bar{r}+r
. By definition,

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r})-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{r}+r}\sigma_{j}^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r})-\mathbf{A}^{*}).

(C.2)

By Mirsky’s singular value inequality
(
Mirsky, 1960
)
,

\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{r}+r}[\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}))-\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{A}^{*})]^{2}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{r}+r}\sigma_{j}^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r})-\mathbf{A}^{*})=\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r})-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(C.3)

As the
\ell_{\infty}
norm of any vector is smaller than the
\ell_{2}
norm, it follows the same upper bound

\max_{1\leq j\leq\bar{r}+r}\left|\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}))-\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\right|\leq\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{\bar{r}+r}\sigma_{j}^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r})-\mathbf{A}^{*})\right\}^{1/2}=\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r})-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}.

(C.4)

For any
1\leq j\leq\bar{r}
, note that
\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}))+s(p,T)=\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{A}^{*})+[\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}))-\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{A}^{*})]+s(p,T)
. For
j>r
,
\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{A}^{*})=0
and
|\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}))-\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{A}^{*})|=o_{p}(s(p,T))
, provided that
\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{d_{\text{RR}}(p,\bar{r})/T}=o(s(p,T))
. Hence,
s(p,T)
is the dominating term in
\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}))+s(p,T)
, when
j>r
.
When
j\leq r
, as
T\to\infty
,
s(p,T)/\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\to 0
and
\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(p,\bar{r}))+s(p,T)\to\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{A}^{*})
.

Hence, for
j>r
, as
T\to\infty
,

\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}))+s(p,T)}{\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}))+s(p,T)}\to\frac{s(p,T)}{s(p,T)}=1.

(C.5)

For
j<r
,

\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}))+s(p,T)}{\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}))+s(p,T)}\to\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\mathbf{A}^{*})}{\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{A}^{*})}.

(C.6)

For
j=r
,

\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}))+s(p,T)}{\sigma_{j}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}))+s(p,T)}\to\frac{s(p,T)}{\sigma_{r}(\mathbf{A}^{*})}=o\left(\min_{1\leq j\leq r-1}\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\mathbf{A}^{*})}{\sigma_{j}(\mathbf{A}^{*})}\right).

(C.7)

∎

Next, we prove the common dimension selection consistency of the BIC.

Proof of Theorem
4
.

To show the consistency of common dimension selection via BIC, it suffices to show that

\min_{0\leq k<d}\text{BIC}(k)-\text{BIC}(d)>0~~\text{and}~~\min_{d<k\leq r}\text{BIC}(k)-\text{BIC}(d)>0.

(C.8)

Consider the under-parameterized case
k<d
first. Note that

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{Y}-\widehat{\mathbf{A}}(d)\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\|\mathbf{E}-(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}(d)-\mathbf{A}^{*})\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&\|\mathbf{E}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}(d)-\mathbf{A}^{*})\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+2\langle\mathbf{E}\mathbf{X}^{\top},\widehat{\mathbf{A}}(d)-\mathbf{A}^{*}\rangle\\
=&T\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})+Cd_{\text{CS}}(p,r,d)+o_{p}(T)=T\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})+o_{p}(T)\end{split}

(C.9)

and

\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{Y}-\widehat{\mathbf{A}}(k)\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}=\|\mathbf{E}-(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}(k)-\mathbf{A}^{*})\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
=&\|\mathbf{E}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|(\widehat{\mathbf{A}}(k)-\mathbf{A}^{*})\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+2\langle\mathbf{E}\mathbf{X}^{\top},\widehat{\mathbf{A}}(k)-\mathbf{A}^{*}\rangle\\
\geq&T\text{tr}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})+CTg_{\min}^{2}+o_{p}(T).\end{split}

(C.10)

Thus, by
\log(1+x)\asymp x
for
x\to 0
, we have

\begin{split}&\min_{0\leq k<d}\text{BIC}(k)-\text{BIC}(d)\\
\asymp&pT\log\left(1+\frac{\|\mathbf{Y}-\widehat{\mathbf{A}}(k)\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}-\|\mathbf{Y}-\widehat{\mathbf{A}}(d)\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}}{\|\mathbf{Y}-\widehat{\mathbf{A}}(d)\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}}\right)\\
\geq&CTg_{\min}^{2}-Cpd\log(T)+o_{p}(T)\end{split}

(C.11)

and it follows that
\mathbb{P}(\min_{0\leq k<d}\text{BIC}(k)-\text{BIC}(d)>0)\to 1
, as
T\to\infty
, provided that
\log(T)pg_{\min}^{-2}/T\to 0
.

For the cases
k>d
, using the similar arguments in the proof of Theorem
2
, we can show that
\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}(k)-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\asymp\sqrt{d_{\text{CS}}(p,r,k)/T}
. It follows that

\begin{split}&\min_{d<k\leq r}\text{BIC}(k)-\text{BIC}(d)\geq O_{p}(p)+p(k-d)\log(T)\end{split}

(C.12)

which implies that
\min_{d<k\leq r}\text{BIC}(k)-\text{BIC}(d)>0
, since
\log(T)\to\infty
as
T\to\infty
.

∎

Appendix D
Supplementary materials for VAR(
\ell
) models

Appendix
D
presents the supplementary materials of modeling, estimation and theory for the VAR(
\ell
). It begins with some preliminaries of tensor notation and tensor operation.

D.1
Some basics of tensor algebra

We follow the notations in
Kolda and Bader, 2009
to denote tensors of order three or higher by Euler script boldface letters, e.g.,
\mathscr{A}
. For a generic
d
-th order tensor
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}
, denote its elements by
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}(i_{1},i_{2},\dots,i_{d})
and unfolding of
\mathscr{A}
along the
n
-mode by
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(n)}
, where the columns of
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(n)}
are the
n
-mode vectors of
\mathscr{A}
, for
n=1,\dots,d
. The Frobenius norm of a tensor
\mathscr{A}
is defined as
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}\|_{\text{F}}=\sqrt{\sum_{i_{1}}\cdots\sum_{i_{d}}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}(i_{1},\dots,i_{d})^{2}}
. The mode-
n
multiplication
\times_{n}
of a tensor
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{1}\times\cdots\times p_{d}}
and a matrix
\mathbf{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{q_{n}\times p_{n}}
is defined as

(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}\times_{n}\mathbf{B})(i_{1},\dots,j_{n}\dots,i_{d})=\sum_{i_{n}=1}^{p_{n}}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}(i_{1},\dots,i_{n},\dots,i_{d})\mathbf{B}(j_{n},i_{n}),

(D.1)

for
n=1,\dots,d
, respectively.

The tensor ranks considered in this paper are defined as the matrix ranks of the unfoldings of
\mathscr{A}
along all modes, namely
\text{rank}_{i}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})=\text{rank}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(i)})
, for
i=1,\dots,d
. If the tensor ranks of
\mathscr{A}
are
r_{1},\dots,r_{d}
, where
1\leq r_{i}\leq p_{i}
, there exists a tensor
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{d}}
and matrices
\mathbf{U}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{p_{i}\times r_{i}}
, such that

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{1}\mathbf{U}_{1}\times_{2}\mathbf{U}_{2}\cdots\times_{d}\mathbf{U}_{d},

(D.2)

which is well known as Tucker decomposition
(
Tucker, 1966
)
. With the Tucker decomposition, the
n
-mode unfolding of
\mathscr{A}
can be written as

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(n)}=\mathbf{U}_{n}\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(n)}(\mathbf{U}_{d}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbf{U}_{n+1}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{n-1}\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathbf{U}_{1})^{\top},

(D.3)

where
\otimes
denotes the Kronecker product for matrices.

D.2
Algorithm, rank selection and common dimension selection for VAR(
\ell
) models

A gradient descent algorithm (Algorithm
3
) is proposed for the estimation of VAR(
\ell
) model.
Note that the loss function with respect to the parameter tensor
\mathscr{A}
is
\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})=(2T)^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(1)}\mathbf{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
, and its gradient has the form of
\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})=T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(1)}\mathbf{x}_{t}-\mathbf{y}_{t})\circ\mathbf{X}_{t}
, where
\circ
denotes the tensor outer product, and
\mathbf{X}_{t}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\dots\mathbf{y}_{t-\ell}\textbf{]}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times\ell}
.
The partial derivatives are listed below,

\begin{split}\nabla_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{L}&=\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})_{(1)}\left[(\mathbf{L}\otimes\mathbf{C})(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{11})_{(1)}^{\top}+(\mathbf{L}\otimes\mathbf{P})(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{12})_{(1)}^{\top}\right]\\
&+\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})_{(2)}\left[(\mathbf{L}\otimes\mathbf{C})(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{11})_{(2)}^{\top}+(\mathbf{L}\otimes\mathbf{R})(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{21})_{(2)}^{\top}\right],\\
\nabla_{\mathbf{R}}\mathcal{L}&=\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})_{(1)}[(\mathbf{L}\otimes\mathbf{C})(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{21})_{(1)}^{\top}+(\mathbf{L}\otimes\mathbf{P})(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{22})_{(1)}^{\top}],\\
\nabla_{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{L}&=\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})_{(2)}[(\mathbf{L}\otimes\mathbf{C})(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{12})_{(2)}^{\top}+(\mathbf{L}\otimes\mathbf{R})(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{22})_{(2)}^{\top}],\\
\nabla_{\mathbf{L}}\mathcal{L}&=\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})_{(3)}(\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\otimes\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]})\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(3)}^{\top},\\
\text{and}~\nabla_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}\mathcal{L}&=\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})\times_{1}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}^{\top}\times_{2}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}\times_{3}\mathbf{L}^{\top},\end{split}

(D.4)

where
\otimes
is the Kronecker product of matrices.

Algorithm 3

Gradient descent algorithm for VAR(
\ell
) model with known
r_{1}
,
r_{2}
,
r_{3}
and
d

1:
Input
:
\mathbf{Y}
,
\mathbf{X}
,
\eta
,
I
,
\mathbf{C}^{(0)}
,
\mathbf{R}^{(0)}
,
\mathbf{P}^{(0)}
,
\mathbf{L}^{(0)}
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(0)}

2:
for

i=0,\dots,I-1

3:
\mathbf{C}^{(i+1)}=\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\eta\nabla_{\mathbf{C}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}-\eta a\big[2\mathbf{C}^{(i)}(\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{d})+\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}+\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\big]

4:
\mathbf{R}^{(i+1)}=\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\eta\nabla_{\mathbf{R}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}-\eta a\big[\mathbf{R}^{(i)}(\mathbf{R}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{1}-d})+\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\big]

5:
\mathbf{P}^{(i+1)}=\mathbf{P}^{(i)}-\eta\nabla_{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}-\eta a\big[\mathbf{P}^{(i)}(\mathbf{P}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{P}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{2}-d})+\mathbf{C}^{(i)}\mathbf{C}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\big]

6:
\mathbf{L}^{(i+1)}=\mathbf{L}^{(i)}-\eta\nabla_{\mathbf{L}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}-\eta a\mathbf{L}^{(i)}(\mathbf{L}^{(i)\top}\mathbf{L}^{(i)}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{3}})

7:

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(i+1)}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(i)}-\eta\nabla_{\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}}\mathcal{L}^{(i)}

8:
end for

9:
Return
:
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(I)}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(I)}\times_{1}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(I)}~\mathbf{R}^{(I)}\textbf{]}\times_{2}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(I)}~\mathbf{P}^{(I)}\textbf{]}\times_{3}\mathbf{L}^{(I)}

For the initialization of Algorithm
3
, we first consider the rank-constrained estimator,

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}$}_{\text{RR}}(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})=\argmin_{\text{rank}(\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{(i)})=r_{i},1\leq i\leq 3}\frac{1}{2T}\|\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(1)}\mathbf{x}_{t}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

(D.5)

and then conduct the HOSVD:
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}$}_{\text{RR}}(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{G}}$}\times_{1}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times_{2}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\times_{3}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{3}
, where
\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{i}
is the top
r_{i}
left singular vectors of its mode-
i
matricization for each
1\leq i\leq 3
.
By applying the method in Section
3.2
to
\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}
, we can obtain
\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}
,
\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}
, and
\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}
, and the initialization can then be set to
\mathbf{C}^{(0)}=b\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}
,
\mathbf{R}^{(0)}=b\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}
,
\mathbf{P}^{(0)}=b\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}
,
\mathbf{L}^{(0)}=b\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{3}
, and
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(0)}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}$}_{\text{RR}}(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})\times_{1}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(0)}~\mathbf{R}^{(0)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\times_{2}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(0)}~\mathbf{P}^{(0)}\textbf{]}^{\top}\times_{3}\mathbf{L}^{(0)\top}
.

To select the tensor ranks of
(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})
, we first set their pre-specified upper bounds,
(\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2},\bar{r}_{3})
, where each
\bar{r}_{i}
is greater than
r_{i}
but much smaller than
p
. The rank-constrained estimator
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}$}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2},\bar{r}_{3})
can then be calculated according to (
D.5
).
As a result, the tensor ranks can be selected by the ridge-type ratio method,

\widehat{r}_{i}=\argmin_{1\leq j\leq\bar{r}_{i}-1}\frac{\sigma_{j+1}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}$}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2},\bar{r}_{3})_{(i)})+s(p,T)}{\sigma_{j}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}$}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2},\bar{r}_{3})_{(i)})+s(p,T)},\hskip 14.22636pt1\leq i\leq 3.

(D.6)

Denote by
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},d)
the estimated parameter tensor from Algorithm
3
with the tensor ranks of
(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})
and common dimension
d
. The BIC of models (
2.9
) and (
2.12
) can be defined as

\text{BIC}(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},d)=Tp\log\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{A}}$}(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},d)\mathbf{x}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\right)+d_{\text{CS}}(p,\ell,r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},d)\log(T),

and we can choose the common dimension by

\widehat{d}=\argmin_{0\leq d\leq\min(r_{1},r_{2})}\text{BIC}(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},d).

Input:
\mathbf{X}
,
\mathbf{Y}
,

\eta
,
a
,
b
and
\bar{r}
or (
\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2},\bar{r}_{3}
)

Initialization and rank selection:

\bullet
initialize
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r})
or
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}$}_{\text{RR}}(\bar{r}_{1},\bar{r}_{2},\bar{r}_{3})

\bullet
select
\widehat{r}
or
(\widehat{r}_{1},\widehat{r}_{2},\widehat{r}_{3})
by ridge-type ratio estimator

\bullet
initialize
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\widehat{r})=\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}\widetilde{\mathbf{S}}\widetilde{\mathbf{V}}^{\top}
or

\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}$}_{\text{RR}}(\widehat{r}_{1},\widehat{r}_{2},\widehat{r}_{3})=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{S}}$}\times_{1}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{1}\times_{2}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{2}\times_{3}\widetilde{\mathbf{U}}_{3}

Return
\widehat{r}
or
(\widehat{r}_{1},\widehat{r}_{2},\widehat{r}_{3})
,
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{\text{RR}}(\widehat{r})
or
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}$}_{\text{RR}}(\widehat{r}_{1},\widehat{r}_{2},\widehat{r}_{3})

Gradient descent and
d
selection:

for
d=0
to
\widehat{r}
or
\min(\widehat{r}_{1},\widehat{r}_{2})

\bullet
initialize
(\mathbf{C}^{(0)},\mathbf{R}^{(0)},\mathbf{P}^{(0)},\mathbf{D}^{(0)})
or

(\mathbf{C}^{(0)},\mathbf{R}^{(0)},\mathbf{P}^{(0)},\mathbf{L}^{(0)},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(0)})

\bullet
estimate via gradient descent

\bullet
calculate
\text{BIC}(\widehat{r},d)
or
\text{BIC}(\widehat{r}_{1},\widehat{r}_{2},\widehat{r}_{3},d)

Return
\widehat{d}
,
(\widehat{\mathbf{A}},\widehat{\mathbf{C}},\widehat{\mathbf{R}},\widehat{\mathbf{P}},\widehat{\mathbf{D}})
or
(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}$},\widehat{\mathbf{C}},\widehat{\mathbf{R}},\widehat{\mathbf{P}},\widehat{\mathbf{L}},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{G}}$})

Output:
\widehat{r}
or
(\widehat{r}_{1},\widehat{r}_{2},\widehat{r}_{3})
,
\widehat{d}
,
(\widehat{\mathbf{A}},\widehat{\mathbf{C}},\widehat{\mathbf{R}},\widehat{\mathbf{P}},\widehat{\mathbf{D}})
or
(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widetilde{A}}$},\widehat{\mathbf{C}},\widehat{\mathbf{R}},\widehat{\mathbf{P}},\widehat{\mathbf{L}},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{\widehat{G}}$})

Figure 5:
Flowchart of the proposed estimation procedure.

D.3
Theoretical results for VAR(
\ell
) models

Proof of Theorem
5
.

The proof consists of two parts. The first part is an extension of Theorem
1
for computational convergence analysis of the gradient descent iterates, provided that some regularity conditions are satisfied. The second part is the statistical analysis to show that these conditions do hold with high probability.

First, similarly to Theorem
1
, for the iterate at the step
i
, define the estimation error of
(\mathbf{C},\mathbf{R},\mathbf{P},\mathbf{L},\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$})
up to the optimal rotations

\begin{split}E^{(i)}=&\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{O}_{c}\in\mathbb{O}^{d\times d},\mathbf{O}_{r}\in\mathbb{O}^{(r_{1}-d)\times(r_{1}-d)},\\
\mathbf{O}_{p}\in\mathbb{O}^{(r_{2}-d)\times(r_{2}-d)},\mathbf{O}_{l}\in\mathbb{O}^{r_{3}\times r_{3}}\end{subarray}}\Big\{\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}^{(i)}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{p}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+\|\mathbf{L}^{(i)}-\mathbf{L}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{l}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(i)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}\times_{1}\text{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{r})\times_{2}\text{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{p})\times\mathbf{O}_{l}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\Big\}\end{split}

(D.7)

and the corresponding optimal rotations as
(\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)},\mathbf{O}_{p}^{(i)},\mathbf{O}_{3}^{(i)})
. Denote
\mathbf{O}_{1}^{(i)}=\text{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},\mathbf{O}_{r}^{(i)})
and
\mathbf{O}_{2}^{(i)}=\text{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c}^{(i)},\mathbf{O}_{p}^{(i)})
.

In a similar fashion, the
\alpha
-RSC and
\beta
-RSS conditions are also assumed for the loss function
\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$})
, and for the given sample, define

\xi(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},d)=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r_{1}},\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r_{2}},\\
\mathbf{L}\in\mathbb{O}^{\ell\times r_{3}},\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times r_{2}\times r_{3}},\|\scalebox{0.7}{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}}\|_{\text{F}}=1\end{subarray}}\big\langle\mathcal{L}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}),\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{1}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\times_{2}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\times_{3}\mathbf{L}\big\rangle.

(D.8)

For simplicity, assume
b=\bar{\sigma}^{1/4}
and
a=C\alpha\alpha_{1}^{3/4}\kappa^{-2}
. For any
i=0,1,2\dots
, we assume that

\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1.1b,~\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1.1b,~\|\mathbf{L}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1.1b,\max_{i=1,2,3}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\leq\frac{1.1\bar{\sigma}}{b^{3}},

(D.9)

and
E^{(i)}\leq C\bar{\sigma}^{1/2}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2}
. In addition, for the initial value
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(0)}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(0)}\times_{1}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(0)}~\mathbf{R}^{(0)}\textbf{]}\times_{2}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(0)}~\mathbf{P}^{(0)}\textbf{]}\times_{3}\mathbf{L}^{(0)}
, we assume that
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(0)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\underline{\sigma}
.

Based on these conditions, we have that

\begin{split}E^{(i+1)}\leq&E^{(i)}-2\eta(Q_{\text{G},1}+Q_{\text{C},1}+Q_{\text{R},1}+Q_{\text{P},1}+Q_{\text{L},1})\\
&+\eta^{2}(Q_{\text{G},2}+Q_{\text{C},2}+Q_{\text{R},2}+Q_{\text{P},2}+Q_{\text{L},2}),\end{split}

(D.10)

where the terms
Q_{\cdot,1}
and
Q_{\cdot,2}
are defined similarly as those in the proof of Theorem
1
. The upper bound for
Q_{\text{G},2}+Q_{\text{C},2}+Q_{\text{R},2}+Q_{\text{P},2}+Q_{\text{L},2}
and the lower bound for
Q_{\text{G},1}+Q_{\text{C},1}+Q_{\text{R},1}+Q_{\text{P},1}+Q_{\text{L},1}
can be derived. Extending these results from the matrix case to the 3rd order tensor case hinges on the framework in the Theorem 3.1 of
Han et al., 2021
, which leads to

\begin{split}E^{(i)}&\leq\left(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2}\right)E^{(i)}+C\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\bar{\sigma}^{-3/2}\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},d)\end{split}

(D.11)

when
\eta=\eta_{0}\beta^{-1}\bar{\sigma}^{-3/2}
for some
\eta_{0}<1/280
.

By induction, we have that for any
t=1,2,\dots
,

E^{(i)}\leq(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{(i)}E^{(i)}+C\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\bar{\sigma}^{-3/2}\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},d).

(D.12)

For the error bound of
\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(i)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}
, by Lemma
12
,

\begin{split}&\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(i)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq C\bar{\sigma}^{3/2}E^{(i)}\\
\leq&C\bar{\sigma}^{3/2}(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{i}E^{(0)}+C\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},d)\\
\leq&C\kappa^{2}(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})^{i}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(0)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+C\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},d).\end{split}

(D.13)

The conditions for iterates
(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(i)},\mathbf{C}^{(i)},\mathbf{R}^{(i)},\mathbf{P}^{(i)},\mathbf{L}^{(i)})
can be verified similarly as in Theorem
1
.

Second, in the statistical analysis, we need to verify the upper bound for the initial value
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(0)}
, the deviation bound for
\xi(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},d)
,
\alpha
-RSC condition and
\beta
-RSS conditions. For the low-rank estimator
\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{\text{RR}}(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})
, according to Theorem 1 of
Wang et al., 2020
, with probability at least
1-\exp[-C(r_{1}r_{2}r_{3}+pr_{1}+pr_{2}+\ell r_{3})]-\exp(-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T)
,

\|\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}}_{\text{RR}}(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\alpha^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\text{RR}}(p,\ell,r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})}{T}}.

(D.14)

Then, by the same techniques in the proof of Lemma
6
, we can obtain that

\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{(0)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\bar{\sigma}^{3/4}\kappa^{2}g_{\min}^{-2}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\text{RR}}(p,\ell,r_{1},r_{2},r_{3})}{T}}\lesssim\underline{\sigma}.

(D.15)

The
\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}
-RSC and
\beta_{\text{RSS}}
-RSS conditions and the deviation bound for
\xi(r_{1},r_{2},r_{3},d)
can be proved to hold with high probability approaching one, in the same manner as Lemmas
3
and
4
. For brevity, the detailed proofs are omitted.

∎

The following lemma is a straightforward extension of Lemma E.2 in
Han et al., 2021
with common subgroup structures included, so the proof is omitted for simplicity.

Lemma 12
.

Suppose that
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}\times_{1}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\times_{2}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\times_{3}\mathbf{L}^{*}
,
\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}=\mathbf{I}_{r_{1}}
,
\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}=\mathbf{I}_{r_{2}}
,

\mathbf{L}^{*\top}\mathbf{L}^{*}=\mathbf{I}_{r_{3}}
,
\bar{\sigma}=\max_{1\leq i\leq 3}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}_{(i)}\|_{\textup{op}}
, and
\underline{\sigma}=\min_{1\leq i\leq 3}\sigma_{r_{i}}(\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}_{(i)}^{*})
. Let
\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}=\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}\times_{1}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\times_{2}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\times_{3}\mathbf{L}
with
\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\|_{\textup{op}}\leq(1+c_{b})b
,
\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\|_{\textup{op}}\leq(1+c_{b})b
,
\|\mathbf{L}\|_{\textup{op}}\leq(1+c_{b})b
and
\max_{1\leq i\leq 3}\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}_{(i)}\|_{\textup{op}}\leq(1+c_{b})\bar{\sigma}/b^{3}
for some constant
c_{b}>0
. Define

\begin{split}E:=&\min_{\mathbf{O}_{1},\mathbf{O}_{2},\mathbf{O}_{3}}\big(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{R}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{1}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}-\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{*}~\mathbf{P}^{*}\textbf{]}\mathbf{O}_{2}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\\
&+\|\mathbf{L}^{(i)}-\mathbf{L}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{3}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{(i)}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{G}$}^{*}\times_{1}\mathbf{O}_{1}\times_{2}\mathbf{O}_{2}\times_{3}\mathbf{O}_{3}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\big).\end{split}

(D.16)

Then, we have

\begin{split}E&\leq\left(7b^{-6}+\frac{12b^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}C_{b}\right)\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+2b^{-2}C_{b}\big(\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{1}}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\\
&+\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}^{\top}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{2}}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{L}^{\top}\mathbf{L}-b^{2}\mathbf{I}_{r_{3}}\|\big),\end{split}

(D.17)

and

\|\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}-\mbox{\boldmath$\mathscr{A}$}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\leq 4b^{6}[1+\bar{\sigma}^{2}b^{-8}(3+2c_{b})^{2}(1+c_{b})^{4}]E,

(D.18)

where
C_{b}=1+7\bar{\sigma}^{2}b^{-8}[(1+c_{b})^{4}+(1+c_{b})^{4}(2+c_{b})^{2}]
.

Appendix E
VAR for diverging eigenvalue effects

E.1
Factor analysis of reduced-rank VAR process

In this section, we conduct factor analysis for the reduced-rank VAR process with diverging eigenvalue effects. For the VAR(1) model
\mathbf{y}_{t}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}
, the covariance matrix of
\mathbf{y}_{t}
satisfies

\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{A}^{\top}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},

(E.1)

where
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}=\text{var}(\mathbf{y}_{t})
and
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}=\text{var}(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t})
.
When the covariance matrix
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}
has some diverging eigenvalues, the singular values of
\mathbf{A}
and/or the eigenvalues of
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
may also be diverging.
If
\mathbf{A}
has diverging singular values, the dynamic autoregressive part has the diverging effect, whereas if the leading eigenvalues of
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
are spiky, the white noise part has the diverging eigenvalue effect.

Note that the stationarity of the VAR process requires that all eigenvalues of
\mathbf{A}
are strictly smaller than one in terms of absolute value. However, this condition can hold with some diverging singular values. For example, consider a
p\times p
coefficient matrix

\mathbf{A}=0.9\mathbf{1}_{p}(1,0,0,0,\cdots,0)^{\top}=\begin{pmatrix}0.9&0&0&\cdots&0\\
0.9&0&0&\cdots&0\\
0.9&0&0&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0.9&0&0&\cdots&0\end{pmatrix}

(E.2)

and it can be verified that
\sigma_{1}(\mathbf{A})=0.9\sqrt{p}
and the nonzero eigenvalue of
\mathbf{A}
is 0.9.

The reduced-rank VAR(1) process of rank
r
can be formulated to the factor model in
Gao and Tsay, 2022
,

\begin{split}\mathbf{y}_{t}&=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}=\mathbf{U}(\mathbf{S}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}+\mathbf{U}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t})+\mathbf{U}_{\perp}(\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t})\equiv\mathbf{U}\mathbf{f}_{t}+\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2t}\end{split}

(E.3)

where
\mathbf{f}_{t}
is the
r
-dimensional dynamic factor and
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2t}
is the
(p-r)
-dimensional white noise. To analyze the properties of this factor model, we first characterize the strength of the dynamic factor and the white noise term.

For simplicity, we assume that all nonzero singular values in
\mathbf{S}
are diverging with a rate of
p^{\delta_{s}}
with some
\delta_{s}\in[0,1/2]
. If
\delta_{s}=0
, all singular values are bounded.

In the factor model in (
E.3
),
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}
is split into the factor component and white noise component. Hence, it is essential to characterize the signal strength in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{U})
and
\mathcal{M}(\mathbf{V})
.
Assume that the first
r
eigenvalues of
\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\mathbf{U}
scale as
p^{\delta_{u}}
with some
\delta_{u}\in[0,1]
. Assume that the first
K
eigenvalues of
\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}
scale as
p^{\delta_{u}^{\prime}}:=p^{1-\delta_{2}}
with some
\delta_{u}^{\prime}\in(0,1]
and
\delta_{2}=1-\delta_{u}^{\prime}
. For the predictor factors, assume that the diverging eigenvalues of
\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
scale as
p^{\delta_{v}}
.

Since

\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{A}^{\top}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{U}^{\top}+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
,
we have

\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{U})\asymp\cdots\lambda_{r}(\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{U})\asymp p^{\delta_{u}}+p^{2\delta_{s}+\delta_{v}},

and

\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{V})\asymp\cdots\asymp\lambda_{r}(\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{V})\asymp p^{\delta_{v}}.

Since
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{f}}=\mathbf{S}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{S}+\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\mathbf{U}
, we have
\lambda_{1}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{f}})\asymp\cdots\asymp\lambda_{r}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{f}})\asymp p^{(2\delta_{s}+\delta_{v})\vee\delta_{u}}:=p^{1-\delta_{1}}
with
\delta_{1}=1-((2\delta_{s}+\delta_{v})\vee\delta_{u})\in[0,1]
.

Moreover, denote the standardized variable
\mathbf{x}_{t}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{f}}^{-1/2}\mathbf{f}_{t}
and
\mathbf{e}_{t}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}}^{-1/2}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2t}
. For any
k\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{t}\mathbf{e}_{t-k}^{\top}]=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{f}}^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{S}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2,t-k}]\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}}^{-1/2}.

(E.4)

Since
\mathbf{y}_{t-1}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t-1}+\mathbf{A}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t-2}+\mathbf{A}^{2}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t-3}+\cdots
, we have

\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{t}\mathbf{e}_{t-1}^{\top}]=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{f}}^{-1/2}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{S}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t-1}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2,t-1}]\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}}^{-1/2}

(E.5)

and
\|\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{t}\mathbf{e}_{t-1}^{\top}]\|_{\text{op}}\asymp p^{\delta_{s}+\delta_{v}/2-(1-\delta_{1})/2}:=p^{\delta_{3}}
.

For any orthonormal matrices
\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r}
, consider the discrepancy measure

D(\mathbf{H}_{1},\mathbf{H}_{2})=\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{r}\text{tr}(\mathbf{H}_{1}\mathbf{H}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{2}\mathbf{H}_{2}^{\top})}.

By the similar arguments as Theorem 3 in
Gao and Tsay, 2022
, we have the following lemma for the loading matrix estimation error upper bounds.

Lemma 13
.

Under the conditions in Theorem
6
, if
p^{\delta_{1}\vee\delta_{2}}T^{-1}=o(1)
and
\delta_{1}\leq\delta_{2}
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-Cp)
,

D(\widehat{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{U})\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{p^{\delta_{1}}}{T}},~~D(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp},\mathbf{U}_{\perp})\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{p^{\delta_{1}}}{T}},~~\text{and}~~D(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}\widehat{\mathbf{K}},\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{K})\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{p^{2\delta_{2}-3\delta_{1}}}{T}}.

(E.6)

Moreover, for any
1\leq t\leq T
, by the triangle inequality,

\begin{split}\|(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{U}-\mathbf{T}_{U})\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}\leq&\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}+\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}\widehat{\mathbf{K}}_{\perp}\widehat{\mathbf{K}}_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{K}_{\perp}\mathbf{K}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}\\
&+\|\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}\widehat{\mathbf{K}}(\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}}^{K})^{-1/2}\widehat{\mathbf{K}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t}-\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{K}(\mathbf{\Lambda}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{2}}^{K})^{-1/2}\mathbf{K}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}.\end{split}

(E.7)

Hence, following Theorem 4 of
Gao and Tsay, 2022
, we have the following lemma for the estimation upper bound for the transformed data.

Lemma 14
.

Under conditions in Lemma
13
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-Cp)
,

\begin{split}\max_{1\leq t\leq T}\|(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{U}-\mathbf{T}_{U})\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim&p^{(1-\delta_{1})/2}\log(T)D(\widehat{\mathbf{U}},\mathbf{U})\\
&+p^{1/2-\delta_{2}}\log(T)D(\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}\widehat{\mathbf{K}},\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{K})\\
\lesssim&\log(T)\left(\sqrt{\frac{p}{T}}+\sqrt{\frac{p^{1-3\delta_{1}}}{T}}\right)\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\log^{2}(T)p}{T}}.\end{split}

(E.8)

E.2
Theoretical analysis of the transformed time series with the true transformation

Suppose that we have the true transformation
\mathbf{T}_{U}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{U}^{\top}+\mathbf{U}_{\perp}\mathbf{T}_{K}\mathbf{U}_{\perp}^{\top}
and the transformed data
\widebar{\mathbf{y}}_{t}=\mathbf{T}_{U}\mathbf{y}_{t}
. Consider the model

\widebar{\mathbf{y}}_{t}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}+\widebar{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{t}

and the loss function
\widebar{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A})=(2T)^{-1}\|\widebar{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}
, where
\widebar{\mathbf{Y}}=[\widebar{\mathbf{y}}_{1},\cdots,\widebar{\mathbf{y}}_{T}]
is the transformed data and
\mathbf{X}
is the original design matrix.

We start with the analysis of RSC and RSS with diverging eigenvalue effects.

Lemma 15
.

Assume the conditions in Theorem
6
hold. For
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{A}^{*}
such that
\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}\asymp\sqrt{p^{1+\delta_{u}-\delta_{v}}/T}
, denote
\mathbf{\Delta}=\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-C\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T]
,

\alpha^{\prime}_{\textup{RSC}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\beta^{\prime}_{\textup{RSS}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2},

where the dependency measurement quantities are defined as
\alpha^{\prime}_{\textup{RSC}}=\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{V}^{*\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{V}^{*})/2
and
\beta^{\prime}_{\textup{RSS}}=3\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{V}^{*\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{V}^{*})/2
.

Proof of Lemma
15
.

Consider the SVD
\mathbf{A}=\mathbf{U}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
and
\mathbf{A}^{*}=\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{S}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top}
. By Lemma
11
,
\|\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}=\|\sin\Theta(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{V}^{*})\|_{\text{F}}\leq\sigma_{r}^{-1}(\mathbf{A}^{*})\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}=p^{-\delta_{s}}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\asymp\sqrt{p^{1+\delta_{u}-\delta_{v}-2\delta_{s}}/T}
, which diverges to zero as
p
goes to infinity.

Starting from the RSS,

\begin{split}&\left\|(\mathbf{U}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{V}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{S}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top})\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\|^{2}_{2}=\left\|(\mathbf{U}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{V}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{S}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top})(\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top}+\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{*}\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{*\top})\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\|^{2}_{2}\\
\leq&2\left\|(\mathbf{U}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{V}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{S}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top})\mathbf{V}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top}\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\|^{2}_{2}+2\left\|(\mathbf{U}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{V}^{\top}-\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{S}^{*}\mathbf{V}^{*\top})\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{*}\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{*\top}\mathbf{y}_{t}\right\|^{2}_{2}\\
\leq&4\underbrace{\left\|(\mathbf{U}\mathbf{S}-\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{S}^{*})(\mathbf{V}^{*\top}\mathbf{y}_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}}_{T_{1t}}+4\underbrace{\left\|(\mathbf{U}\mathbf{S}-\mathbf{U}^{*}\mathbf{S}^{*})(\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{*})(\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{*\top}\mathbf{y}_{t})\right\|_{2}^{2}}_{T_{2t}}\end{split}

As in Lemma
3
, for
T_{1t}
we have for any
\mathbf{\Delta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-C\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T]

\alpha^{\prime}_{\text{RSC}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|\mathbf{\Delta}(\mathbf{V}^{*\top}\mathbf{y}_{t})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\beta^{\prime}_{\text{RSS}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

(E.9)

where
\alpha^{\prime}_{\text{RSC}}=\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{V}^{*\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{V}^{*})/2\asymp p^{\delta_{v}}
and
\beta^{\prime}_{\text{RSS}}=3\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{V}^{*\top}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{y}}\mathbf{V}^{*})/2\asymp p^{\delta_{v}}
.

Similarly, for
T_{2t}
, for any
\mathbf{\Delta}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times r}
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-C\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T]
,

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|\mathbf{\Delta}(\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{*})(\mathbf{V}_{\perp}^{*\top}\mathbf{y}_{t})\|_{2}^{2}\asymp\frac{p^{1+\delta_{u}-\delta_{v}-2\delta_{s}}}{T}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

(E.10)

and it is a much smaller than the sum of
T_{1t}
given that
T\gtrsim p^{1+\delta_{u}-2\delta_{v}-2\delta_{s}}
, which is confirmed by the sample size requirement of Theorem
6
.

∎

Based on the transformed data
\widebar{\mathbf{y}}_{t}=\mathbf{A}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}+\widebar{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{t}
, the statistical error becomes

\begin{split}\widebar{\xi}(r,d)&=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}},\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r},\\
