\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\textup{F}}=1\end{subarray}}\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}}\mathbf{D}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}^{\top}\right\rangle\\
&=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}},\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r},\\
\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\textup{F}}=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\langle\widebar{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}}\mathbf{D}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}^{\top}\right\rangle\end{split}

(E.11)

Similarly to Lemma
4
, we have the following lemma for the transformed deviation bound.

Lemma 16
.

Assume conditions in Theorem
6
hold. If
T\gtrsim\max(\tau^{2},\tau^{4})p
, then, with probability at least
1-\exp(-Cp)
,

\widebar{\xi}(r,d)\lesssim\tau^{2}M_{1}^{\prime}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\textup{CS}}(p,r,d)}{T}},

where
M_{1}^{\prime}=p^{(\delta_{u}+\delta_{v})/2}
.

We can prove Lemma
16
by using the fact
\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\widebar{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}})\asymp p^{\delta_{u}}
, Lemma
15
, and the same
\epsilon
-net construction method as in the proof of Lemma
4
. The detailed proof is also omitted.

E.3
Analysis of transformed VAR process with estimated transformation

Finally, we consider the estimation error analysis for the estimated data
\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}
with the estimated transformation matrix.
Denote the estimated transformation matrix by
\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{U}=\widehat{\mathbf{U}}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}^{\top}+\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{K}\widehat{\mathbf{U}}_{\perp}^{\top}
and the corresponding data
\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{t}=\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{U}\mathbf{y}_{t}=(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{U}-\mathbf{T}_{U})\mathbf{y}_{t}+\mathbf{T}_{U}(\mathbf{A}^{*}\mathbf{x}_{t}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t})=(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{U}-\mathbf{T}_{U})\mathbf{y}_{t}+\mathbf{A}^{*}\mathbf{x}_{t}+\widebar{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{t}
.
Denote the loss function with the estimated transformation as

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A})=\frac{1}{2T}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2},

(E.12)

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}=[\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{1},\cdots,\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{T}]
is the response matrix with the estimated transformation.

The deviation bound for the estimated data is defined as

\begin{split}\widetilde{\xi}(r,d)&=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}},\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r},\\
\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\textup{F}}=1\end{subarray}}\left\langle\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}}\mathbf{D}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}^{\top}\right\rangle\\
&=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}},\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r},\\
\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\textup{F}}=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\langle[\widebar{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{t}+(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{U}-\mathbf{T}_{U})\mathbf{y}_{t}]\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}}\mathbf{D}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}^{\top}\right\rangle\\
&\leq\widebar{\xi}(r,d)+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}},\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r},\\
\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\textup{F}}=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{U}-\mathbf{T}_{U})\mathbf{y}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}}\mathbf{D}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}^{\top}\rangle:=T_{1}+T_{2}.\end{split}

(E.13)

The upper bound of
T_{1}
is established in Lemma
16
. In addition, by Lemma
14
, with probability at least
1-\exp(-Cp)
,

\max_{1\leq t\leq T}\|(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{U}-\mathbf{T}_{U})\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}\lesssim\tau^{2}\sqrt{r}\sqrt{\frac{\log(T)^{2}p}{T}}:=B.

Then, conditioning on this upper bound, for any
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
such that
\|\mathbf{M}\|_{\text{F}}=1
,

\langle(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{U}-\mathbf{T}_{U})\mathbf{y}_{t}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}^{\top},\mathbf{M}\rangle\leq\|(\widehat{\mathbf{T}}_{U}-\mathbf{T}_{U})\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{M}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\|_{2}\leq B\|\mathbf{M}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\|_{2},

(E.14)

and by Cauchy’s inequality and Lemma
15
, with probability approaching one,

\begin{split}T_{2}&\leq\frac{B}{T}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}},\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r},\\
\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\textup{F}}=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}}\mathbf{D}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\|_{2}\\
&\leq B\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}},\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r},\\
\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\textup{F}}=1\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textup{\bf{]}}\mathbf{D}\textup{\bf{[}}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textup{\bf{]}}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}\|_{2}^{2}}\\
&\leq B\sqrt{\beta_{\text{RSS}}^{\prime}}\asymp\tau^{2}\sqrt{r}\sqrt{\frac{\log^{2}(T)p^{1+\delta_{v}}}{T}}\end{split}

(E.15)

Combining the upper bounds for
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
, we have that with probability approaching one,

\widetilde{\xi}(r,d)\lesssim\tau^{2}p^{\delta^{*}}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\text{CS}}(p,r,d)}{T}},

where
\delta^{*}=(\delta_{u}+\delta_{v})/2
.

Finally, we conclude this appendix by providing the proof of Theorem
6
.

Proof of Theorem
6
.

According to Theorem
1
, when we input the transformed response
\widetilde{\mathbf{Y}}
and the original predictor, after
I
-th iteration with
I\gtrsim\log(p^{\delta_{s}/3}g_{\min})/\log(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha_{\text{RSC}}\beta_{\text{RSS}}^{-1})
,

\|\mathbf{A}^{(I)}-\mathbf{A}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim(\alpha^{\prime}_{\text{RSC}})^{-1}\widetilde{\xi}(r,d)^{2},

where
\widetilde{\xi}(r,d)
is defined above, given the
\alpha^{\prime}_{\text{RSC}}
-RSC and
\beta^{\prime}_{\text{RSS}}
-RSS conditions satisfied.

By Lemma
15
, the RSC and RSS conditions hold with probability at least
1-2\exp[-C(M_{2}^{\prime})^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T]
. Conditioning on RSC and RSS conditions, by Lemmas
13
,
14
and
16
,

\widetilde{\xi}(r,d)\lesssim\tau^{2}p^{(\delta_{u}+\delta_{v})/2}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\text{CS}}(p,r,d)}{T}},

with probability at least
1-C\exp(-Cp)
. Combining these two results, as
\alpha^{\prime}_{\text{RSC}}\asymp p^{\delta_{v}}
, we can obtain the desired result.

∎

Appendix F
Supplementary materials for sparse estimation

F.1
Proof of Theorem
7

Proof.

The proof of Theorem
7
consists of two steps. In the first step, we establish the computational convergence result as an extension of Theorem
1
. In the second step, a statistical convergence analysis is given to verify the sparsity-constrained restricted strong convexity and smoothness conditions.

Step 1.
(Computational convergence analysis)

First, we state some essential notations and conditions. Similarly to Theorem
1
, define the combined estimation errors of
(\mathbf{C}^{(i)},\mathbf{R}^{(i)},\mathbf{P}^{(i)},\mathbf{D}^{(i)})
up to the optimal rotations

\begin{split}E^{(i)}=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{O}_{c}\in\mathbb{O}^{d\times d}\\
\mathbf{O}_{r},\mathbf{O}_{p}\in\mathbb{O}^{(r-d)\times(r-d)}\end{subarray}}&\Big\{\|\mathbf{C}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{R}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}^{(i)}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{p}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+\left\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}-\text{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{r})^{\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\text{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{p})\right\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\Big\}\end{split}

(F.1)

and the estimation errors of
(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{(i)},\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}^{(i)},\mathbf{D}^{(i)})
before the hard thresholding as

\begin{split}\widetilde{E}^{(i)}=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{O}_{c}\in\mathbb{O}^{d\times d}\\
\mathbf{O}_{r},\mathbf{O}_{p}\in\mathbb{O}^{(r-d)\times(r-d)}\end{subarray}}&\Big\{\|\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{(i)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{(i)}-\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+\|\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}^{(i)}-\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{p}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\\
&+\left\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}-\text{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{r})^{\top}\mathbf{D}^{*}\text{diag}(\mathbf{O}_{c},\mathbf{O}_{p})\right\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\Big\}.\end{split}

(F.2)

We assume that for the given sample size
T
,
\mathcal{L}
is restricted strongly convex with parameter
\alpha
and restricted strongly smooth with parameter
\beta
, such that for any rank-
r
matrices
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{A}^{\prime}
where
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{A}^{\top}\in\mathbb{S}(p,p,s_{c}+s_{r})
, and
\mathbf{A}^{\prime},(\mathbf{A}^{\prime})^{\top}\in\mathbb{S}(p,p,s_{c}+s_{p})
,

\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A})-\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime})-\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime}),\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\rangle\leq\frac{\beta}{2}\|\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}.

(F.3)

In addition, for
\mathbf{s}=(s_{c},s_{r},s_{p})
, we denote

\xi(r,d,\mathbf{s})=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]},\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}\in\mathbb{O}^{p\times r}\\
\mathbf{C}\in\mathbb{S}(p,d,s_{c}),\mathbf{R}\in\mathbb{S}(p,r-d,s_{r}),\mathbf{P}\in\mathbb{S}(p,r-d,s_{p})\end{subarray}}\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{*}),\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}^{\top}\textbf{]}\right\rangle.

(F.4)

Moreover, similarly to the proof of Theorem
1
, we assume
b=\sigma_{1}^{1/3}
and
a=C\alpha\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\kappa^{-2}
. For any
i=0,1,2,\dots
, we assume that

\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{R}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1.1b,\|\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{(i)}~\mathbf{P}^{(i)}\textbf{]}\|_{\text{op}}\leq 1.1b,~\text{and}~\|\mathbf{D}^{(i)}\|_{\text{op}}\leq\frac{1.1\sigma_{1}}{b^{2}}.

(F.5)

In addition, we assume that for any
i=0,1,2,\dots
,
E^{(i)}\leq C\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2}
.

As
\mathbf{C}^{(i)}=\text{HT}(\widetilde{\mathbf{C}}^{(i)},s_{c})
,
\mathbf{R}^{(i)}=\text{HT}(\widetilde{\mathbf{R}}^{(i)},s_{r})
,
\mathbf{P}^{(i)}=\text{HT}(\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}^{(i)},s_{c})
,
\mathbf{C}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{c}\in\mathbb{S}(p,d,s_{c}^{*})
,
\mathbf{R}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{r}\in\mathbb{S}(p,r-d,s_{r}^{*})
, and
\mathbf{P}^{*}\mathbf{O}_{p}\in\mathbb{S}(p,r-d,s_{p}^{*})
, by Lemma
17
,

E^{(i)}\leq(1+2\gamma^{-1/2})\widetilde{E}^{(i)}.

(F.6)

Following the same arguments in the proof of Theorem
1
, as in (
A.63
), we have the recursive arguments

\widetilde{E}^{(i+1)}\leq\left(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2}\right)E^{(i)}+C\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}\xi^{2}(r,d,\mathbf{s}),

(F.7)

where the statistical error is replaced with
\xi(r,d,\mathbf{s})
.

Combining (
F.6
), we have

E^{(i+1)}\leq(1+2\gamma^{-1/2})(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha\beta^{-1}\kappa^{-2})E^{(i)}+C\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}\xi^{2}(r,d,\mathbf{s}).

(F.8)

When
\gamma\gtrsim\alpha^{-2}\beta^{2}\kappa^{4}
, it implies that

E^{(i+1)}\leq(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha^{2}\beta^{-2}\kappa^{-4})E^{(i)}+C\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}\xi^{2}(r,d,\mathbf{s}).

(F.9)

By induction, we have that for any
i=1,2,\dots
,

E^{(i)}\leq(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha^{2}\beta^{-2}\kappa^{-4})^{i}E^{(0)}+C\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\sigma_{1}^{-4/3}\xi^{2}(r,d,\mathbf{s}).

(F.10)

Furthermore, by Lemma
2
,

\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}\leq C\kappa^{2}(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha^{2}\beta^{-2}\kappa^{-4})^{i}\|\mathbf{A}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}+C\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}(r,d,\mathbf{s}).

(F.11)

Lastly, similarly to Step 5 in the proof of Theorem
1
, we can verify the essential conditions, except for RSC and RSS conditions, hold recursively, which concludes the computational convergence analysis.

Step 2.
(Statistical convergence analysis)

First, we establish the initialization error bound. By Lemma
20
, with probability approaching one, the estimation error of the initial estimator
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{L_{1}}
is

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{L_{1}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}\lesssim\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{S^{*}\log(p)}{T}},

(F.12)

where
S^{*}=(s_{c}^{*}+s_{r}^{*})(s_{c}^{*}+s_{p}^{*})
.

By Lemma
17
and the same arguments in the proof of Lemma
6
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-2},\tau^{-4})T]-\exp[-C\log(p)]
,

\|\mathbf{A}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}\lesssim\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\kappa^{2}g_{\min}^{-2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{L_{1}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\text{F}}\lesssim\sigma_{1}^{2/3}\kappa^{2}g_{\min}^{-2}\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{S^{*}\log(p)}{T}}.

(F.13)

By the computational convergence results in Step 1, we have that, for all
i=1,2,\dots
,

\|\mathbf{A}^{(i)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\lesssim\kappa^{2}(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha^{2}\beta^{-2}\kappa^{-4})^{i}\|\mathbf{A}^{(0)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\alpha^{-2}\xi^{2}(r,d,\mathbf{s}).

(F.14)

Combining the initialization upper bound in (
F.13
), the
\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}
-RSC and
\beta_{\textup{RSS}}
-RSS conditions in Lemma
18
, and the deviation bound in Lemma
19
, we have that after
I
-th iteration with

I\gtrsim\frac{\log(\kappa^{-1}\sigma_{1}^{-1/3}g_{\min})}{\log(1-C\eta_{0}\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{2}\beta_{\text{RSS}}^{-2}\kappa^{-4})}

(F.15)

with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T]-2\exp[-C\log(p)]
, it holds that

\|\mathbf{A}^{(I)}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}\lesssim\kappa\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{sr+r^{2}+s\min[\log(p),\log(ep/s)]}{T}}

(F.16)

where
s=\max(s_{c}+s_{r},s_{c}+s_{p})
.

∎

F.2
Auxiliary lemmas

The following lemma establishes an upper bound for the hard thresholding operation. This technique is Lemma 3.3 in
Li et al., 2016
. To make the proof self-contained, it is presented here.

Lemma 17
.

Let
\textup{HT}(\cdot,k):\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a hard thresholding operator that keeps the largest
k
entries setting other entries to zero. For
k>k^{*}
,
\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\|\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|_{0}\leq k^{*}
, and any vector
\boldsymbol{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\|\textup{HT}(\boldsymbol{\theta},k)-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\left(1+\frac{2\sqrt{k^{*}}}{\sqrt{k-k^{*}}}\right)\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}.

(F.17)

Next, we prove the restricted strong convexity (RSC) and restricted strong smoothness (RSS) conditions. For the least squares loss function
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A})=(2T)^{-1}\|\mathbf{Y}-\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X}\|_{\text{F}}^{2}
, it is easy to check that for any
\mathbf{A},\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
,

\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A})-\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime})-\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{A}^{\prime}),\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}\rangle=\frac{1}{2T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|(\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime})\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}.

(F.18)

Lemma 18
.

For
\mathbf{A}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}
and
\mathbf{A}^{\prime}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}~\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}^{\prime}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}~\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\textbf{]}^{\top}
, where
\mathbf{C},\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}(p,d,s_{c})
,
\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}(p,r-d,s_{r})
,
\mathbf{P},\mathbf{P}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}(p,r-d,s_{p})
, let
\mathbf{\Delta}=\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{\prime}
. Under conditions in Theorem
7
, if
T\gtrsim M_{2}^{-2}\max(\tau^{4},\tau^{2})\{sr+r^{2}+s\min[\log(p),\log(ep/s)]\}
, then with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T)]

\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\mathbf{y}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\beta_{\textup{RSS}}\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\textup{F}}^{2},

(F.19)

where
s=\max(s_{c}+s_{r},s_{c}+s_{p})
,
\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}=\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})/(2\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A}))
, and
\beta_{\textup{RSS}}=(3\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}))/(2\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A}))
.

Proof of Lemma
18
.

Note that
\mathbf{\Delta}=\mathbf{A}-\mathbf{A}^{*}
admits the matrix decomposition

\mathbf{\Delta}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{\Delta}_{c}~\mathbf{\Delta}_{r}\textbf{]}\mathbf{\Delta}_{d}\textbf{[}\mathbf{\Delta}_{c}~\mathbf{\Delta}_{p}\textbf{]}^{\top}

(F.20)

where
\mathbf{\Delta}_{c}\in\mathbb{S}(p,2d,2s_{c})
,
\mathbf{\Delta}_{r}\in\mathbb{S}(p,2(r-d),2s_{r})
,
\mathbf{\Delta}_{p}\in\mathbb{S}(p,2(r-d),2s_{p})
, and
\mathbf{\Delta}_{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{2r\times 2r}
.

For simplicity in presentation, we consider that
\|\mathbf{\Delta}\|_{\textup{F}}=1
.
By Lemma
7
, for any matrix
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
such that
\|\mathbf{M}\|_{\textup{F}}=1
,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}[|R_{T}(\mathbf{M})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{M})|\geq t]\\
\leq&2\exp\left(-\min\left(\frac{t^{2}}{\tau^{4}T\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})},\frac{t}{\tau^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda^{2}_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})}\right)\right),\end{split}

(F.21)

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}
is defined as

\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{I}_{p}&\mathbf{A}^{*}&\mathbf{A}^{*2}&\mathbf{A}^{*3}&\dots&\mathbf{A}^{*(T-1)}&\dots\\
\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{I}_{p}&\mathbf{A}^{*}&\mathbf{A}^{*2}&\dots&\mathbf{A}^{*(T-2)}&\dots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\dots\\
\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\mathbf{O}&\dots&\mathbf{I}_{p}&\dots\end{bmatrix}.

(F.22)

It remains to find the covering number for the set of
\mathbf{\Delta}
admitting matrix decomposition as in (
F.20
). Based on the row-wise sparsity structure on
\textbf{[}\mathbf{\Delta}_{s}~\mathbf{\Delta}_{r}\textbf{]}
and
\textbf{[}\mathbf{\Delta}_{s}~\mathbf{\Delta}_{p}\textbf{]}
, the nonzero entries in
\mathbf{\Delta}
can be summarized into a submatrix
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Delta}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2(s_{c}+s_{r})\times 2(s_{c}+s_{p})}
that is of rank at most
2r
.

For any given row-wise and column-wise sparsity index sets
\mathcal{S}_{r}
and
\mathcal{S}_{c}
for
\mathbf{\Delta}
, following Lemma
8
, the
\epsilon
-covering number of the low-rank set is
(24/\epsilon)^{4(2s_{c}+s_{r}+s_{p})r+4r^{2}}
. Denote
s=\max(s_{c}+s_{r},s_{c}+s_{p})
. As
\binom{p}{s}\leq\min[p^{s},(ep/s)^{s}]
, for any constant
\epsilon<1
, taking a union bound on the
\epsilon
-covering net for the set of
\widetilde{\mathbf{\Delta}}
and another union bound on
\mathcal{S}_{r}
and
\mathcal{S}_{c}
, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}&\left[\sup_{\mathbf{\Delta}}|R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})|\geq t\right]\leq C\exp\Bigg(2s\min[\log(p),\log(ep/s)]+2sr+r^{2}\\
&-\min\left(\frac{t^{2}}{\tau^{4}T\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})},\frac{t}{\tau^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{2}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda^{2}_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})}\right)\Bigg).\end{split}

(F.23)

Letting
t=T\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma}_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})/2
, for
T\gtrsim M_{2}^{-2}\max(\tau^{4},\tau^{2})\{sr+r^{2}+s\min[\log(p),\log(ep/s)]\}
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\mathbf{\Delta}}|R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})-\mathbb{E}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})|\geq T\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{\Sigma_{\varepsilon}})\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})/2\lambda\right]\\
2&\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T)].\end{split}

(F.24)

Therefore, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-CM_{2}^{2}\min(\tau^{-4},\tau^{-2})T)]
,
\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}\leq T^{-1}R_{T}(\mathbf{\Delta})\leq\beta_{\textup{RSS}}
. Finally, we may replace
\lambda_{\max}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})
and
\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}^{\top})
with
1/\mu_{\min}(\mathcal{A})
and
1/\mu_{\max}(\mathcal{A})
, as in the proof of Lemma
3
.

∎

The following lemma is the deviation bound for the sparsity-constrained set. The proof of this lemma essentially follows that of Lemma
4
, and the covering number of the sparsity-constrained low-rank set has been established in Lemma
18
. The proof is hence omitted for simplicity.

Lemma 19
.

If
T\gtrsim M_{2}^{-2}\max(\tau^{4},\tau^{2})\{sr+r^{2}+s\min[\log(p),\log(ep/s)]\}
, under the conditions in Theorem
7
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-C\log(p)]
,

\xi(r,d,\mathbf{s})\leq\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{sr+r^{2}+s\min[\log(p),\log(ep/s)]}{T}},

(F.25)

where
s=\max(s_{c}+s_{r},s_{c}+s_{p})
.

The last lemma is the estimation error of
L_{1}
regularized estimator
(
Basu and Michailidis, 2015
)
in which the Gaussian distribution condition is relaxed to the sub-Gaussian condition in Assumption
2
.

Lemma 20
.

If
T\gtrsim M_{2}^{-2}\max(\tau^{4},\tau^{2})S^{*}\log(p)
and
\lambda\asymp\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{S^{*}\log(p)/T}
, under the conditions in Theorem
7
, with probability at least
1-2\exp[-C\log(p)]
,

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{A}}_{L_{1}}-\mathbf{A}^{*}\|_{\textup{F}}\lesssim\alpha_{\textup{RSC}}^{-1}\tau^{2}M_{1}\sqrt{\frac{S^{*}\log(p)}{T}},

(F.26)

where
S^{*}=(s_{c}^{*}+s_{r}^{*})(s_{c}^{*}+s_{p}^{*})
.

Appendix G
Additional simulation results

We also conduct a simulation experiment to compare the proposed model with the dynamic factor model due to their close relationship in Section
2.1
. Three data generating processes are used with the dimension
p=50
.

•

DGP1: Dynamic factor model with
r=3
, i.e.

\mathbf{y}_{t}=\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{f}_{t}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}
and
\mathbf{f}_{t}=\mathbf{B}\mathbf{f}_{t-1}+\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t}
, where loading matrix
\mathbf{\Lambda}\in\mathbb{O}^{50\times 3}
is orthonormal,
\mathbf{B}=\text{diag}\{0.8,0.8,0.8\}\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}
,
\{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\}
are
i.i.d.
with
N(\mathbf{0}_{50},0.5\mathbf{I}_{50})
, and
\{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{t}\}
are
i.i.d.
with
N(\mathbf{0}_{3},\mathbf{I}_{3})
.

•

DGP2: The same as in DGP1 except that
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}=\mathbf{\Gamma}\mathbf{e}_{t}
, where
\mathbf{\Gamma}\in\mathbb{O}^{50\times 47}
,
\mathbf{\Gamma}^{\top}\mathbf{\Lambda}=\mathbf{0}_{47\times 3}
, and
\{\mathbf{e}_{t}\}
are
i.i.d.
with
N(\mathbf{0}_{47},0.5\mathbf{I}_{47})
.

•

DGP3: The proposed model in (
2.7
) with rank
r=3
and common dimension of
d=1
, i.e.
\mathbf{y}_{t}=\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{R}\textbf{]}\mathbf{D}\textbf{[}\mathbf{C}~\mathbf{P}\textbf{]}^{\top}\mathbf{y}_{t-1}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}
, where
\mathbf{C}\in\mathbb{O}^{50\times 1}
,
\mathbf{R},\mathbf{P}\in\mathbb{O}^{50\times 2}
,
\mathbf{C}^{\top}\mathbf{R}=\mathbf{C}^{\top}\mathbf{P}=\mathbf{0}_{1\times 2}
,
\mathbf{D}=\text{diag}\{1,1,1\}\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3}
, and
\{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{t}\}
are
i.i.d.
with
N(\mathbf{0}_{50},\mathbf{I}_{50})
.

Note that DGP1 is a standard dynamic factor model, and the series
\{\mathbf{y}_{t}\}
admits a VARMA(1,1) form; see also
Wang et al., 2022
.
As discussed in Section
2.1
, DGP2 is a special dynamic factor model
(
Gao and Tsay, 2022
)
, and it is equivalent to our model with the common dimension
d=3
, i.e., the response and predictor spaces are identical.
In DGP3, we have
d<r
making it fundamentally different from dynamic factor models.

We apply two different modeling frameworks, denoted by VAR-CS and DFM-VAR for convenience, to all the above three data generating processes.
The VAR-CS framework is a VAR(1) model with a common subspace, i.e., the proposed model in (
2.7
), and we adopt the proposed methods for estimation, where the upper bound of ranks
\bar{r}
and hyper-parameter
s(p,T)
are set as those in the first experiment.
For the DFM-VAR framework, we estimate
\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}
via eigen-decomposition of autocovariance matrices and obtain the estimated factors
\widehat{\mathbf{f}}_{t}
.
Then a VAR(1) model is assumed for the factors, and the ordinary least squares method is used in estimation.
It is noteworthy that the VAR-CS framework is misspecified for DGP1, while the DFM-VAR framework is misspecified for DGP3.

From Section
2.1
, the proposed model also admits a form of factor models, and the corresponding factor space is exactly the response factor space.
This motivates us first to compare the estimated factor space and response factor space from the DFM-VAR and VAR-CS frameworks, respectively, and their estimation errors can be defined as
\|\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}^{\top}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}
and
\|\textbf{[}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}~\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\textbf{]}\textbf{[}\widehat{\mathbf{C}}~\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\textbf{]}^{\top}-\mathbf{\Lambda}\mathbf{\Lambda}^{\top}\|_{\text{F}}
, respectively.
The median and quartiles of errors are plotted in the upper panel of Figure
6
, and the VAR-CS framework has a worse performance for DGP1, but a better performance for DGP3. This is due to the fact that DGP1 is a VARMA(1,1) model in nature, while a VAR(1) model is used in the VAR-CS framework. Moreover, although DGP3 admits a form of static factor models, its low-dimensional structure is totally different from the VAR model used in the DFM-VAR framework.
Finally, for DGP2, the two modeling frameworks are both correctly specified, while our framework has a slightly, but uniformly, better performance.

We next evaluate the prediction performance of the two modeling frameworks.
The prediction errors are measured by
\|\widehat{\mathbf{y}}_{T+1}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{y}_{T+1}|\mathcal{F}_{T}]\|_{2}
, and the one-step-ahead prediction of our framework is
\widehat{\mathbf{y}}_{T+1}=\widehat{\mathbf{A}}\mathbf{y}_{T}
, where
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}
is the fitted parameter matrix.
For the DFM-VAR framework, the prediction is defined as
\widehat{\mathbf{y}}_{T+1}=\widehat{\mathbf{\Lambda}}\widehat{\mathbf{f}}_{T+1}
, while
\widehat{\mathbf{f}}_{T+1}
is predicted by the fitted low-dimensional VAR(1) model.
The lower panel of Figure
6
plots the median and quartiles of prediction errors from 500 replications.
Interestingly, for both DGP1 and DGP2, our modeling framework has almost the same prediction errors as those of DFM-VAR, even though model misspecification occurs in DGP1 for our modeling framework.
More importantly, for DGP3, the low-dimensional response and predictor spaces are distinct, and hence the dynamic factor modeling has much worse performance in prediction.
From the results, it is advantageous to use the proposed model in high-dimensional time series forecasting, which is the main task in the literature.

Figure 6:
Estimation errors of factor spaces (upper panel) and prediction errors (lower panel) from the proposed methodology (VAR-CS) and dynamic factor modeling (DFM-VAR).

Appendix H
Information of macroeconomic dataset

The information of forty macroeconomic variables is given in the Table
E.1
. All variables are transformed to be stationary with codes given in column T, and except for financial variables, all variables are subject to seasonal adjustments. The US macroeconomic data set is originally from
Stock and Watson, 2009
, and these forty economic variables are selected from
Koop, 2013
.

Table E.1:
Forty quarterly macroeconomic variables belonging to 8 categories. Category code (C) represents: 1 = GDP and its decomposition, 2 = national association of purchasing managers (NAPM) indices, 3 = industrial production, 4 = housing, 5 = money, credit, interest rates, 6 = employment, 7 = prices and wages, 8 = others. Variables are seasonally adjusted except for those in category 5. All variables are transformed to stationarity with the following transformation codes (T): 1 = no transformation, 2 = first difference, 3 = second difference, 4 = log, 5 = first difference of logged variables, 6 = second difference of logged variables.
